<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{Thm1Intro}
	Assume that\footnote{ 
The assumption $p\leq2$ is quite natural. Otherwise, in view of the
classical embedding $\dot{W}^{1,p}\subset C$ if $p>2$, we would impose continuity on the observed image, which is not very interesting.} $p\in(1,2]$ and $\gamma = 2-\frac{2}{p}$. Then there exists a constant $C > 0$, which is independent of $f$, such that 
\begin{equation}
|\bar{E}-\bar{E}_n|^{\frac{1}{2}} + \Vert\bar{u}-\bar{u}_{n}\Vert_{L^{2}}\leq C K(n^{-\gamma},f;L^{2},\dot {W} 
^{1,p})  \label{enlaprimera}
\end{equation}
for every $n \in \mathbb{N}$. In addition, if\footnote{In light of \cite[Proposition 1 and Remark 3]{BIM},  the assumption $\bar{u} \in L^\infty$ is automatically satisfied for a general datum $f \in L^2$. However, it is not possible to provide an estimate of type $\|\bar{u}\|_{L^\infty} \leq c \|f \|_{L^2}$.} $\bar{u} \in \dot{\text{BV}} \cap L^\infty$ then
\begin{equation}\label{29.9.1}
	\Vert\bar{u}-\bar{u}_{n}\Vert_{L^{2}}^2 \leq  C n^{-1/2} K(n^{-\gamma},f;L^{2},\dot {W} 
^{1,p}) (\|\bar{u}\|_{L^\infty} \emph{TV}(\bar{u}))^{1/2}.  
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{Thm2Intro} Assume that $p \in(1, 2)$ and $\alpha\in\big(0, 2-\frac{2}{p 
} \big)$. Let 
\begin{equation*}
\beta= \frac{\alpha}{2-\frac{2}{p}}, \qquad\frac{1}{q} = \frac{1-\beta}{2} + 
\frac{\beta}{p}, \qquad\frac{1}{\gamma} = \frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}. 
\end{equation*}
If $f \in\dot{B}^{\beta}_{q}(\gamma, \infty)$ then 
\begin{equation}  \label{25.3Nov}
\|\bar{u}-\bar{u}_{n}\|_{L^{2}} \lesssim \begin{cases} n^{-\alpha} \|f\|_{\dot{B}^{\beta}_{q}(\gamma, \infty)} & \qquad \text{if}  \qquad \alpha \geq \frac{1}{2}, \\
n^{-\frac{2 \alpha +1}{4}}   \|f\|_{\dot{B}^{\beta}_{q}(\gamma, \infty)}^{1/2} (\|\bar{u}\|_{L^\infty} \emph{TV}(\bar{u}))^{1/4} & \qquad \text{if} \qquad \alpha < \frac{1}{2}, 
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
for all $n \in \mathbb{N}.$ In particular, if $f \in\dot{W}^{\beta, q}$ then \eqref{25.3Nov} holds.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
Since the appearance of the works \cite{BVY, BVY2, BSVSY}, the spaces $\dot{B}^\beta_q(\gamma, \infty)$ have attracted  a great deal of attention due to several important reasons; cf. the survey paper \cite{BSVSY2}.  
We should also mention   the earlier work of Bourgain and Nguyen  \cite{BoN, N2}, where a  distinguished  case of parameters was already considered. In particular,  it was shown as a combination of  \cite{BoN, N2, BVY, GY, BSVSY, BSVSY2} that these spaces provide     derivative-free characterizations of standard Sobolev (semi)norms, more precisely, for $\gamma \neq 0$, 
\begin{equation}\label{2.9.3}
\dot{B}^{\beta}_{q}(\gamma,\infty) (\mathbb{R}^N)
=
\begin{cases}
\dot{W}^{1,q}(\mathbb{R}^N) & \qquad  \text{if} \qquad  \beta=1, \qquad q \in (1, \infty), \\
\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^N) & \qquad \text{if} \qquad \beta = q = 1, \qquad \gamma \in \mathbb{R} \backslash [-1, 0], \\
L^q(\mathbb{R}^N) &\qquad  \text{if} \qquad  \beta=0, \qquad q \in [1, \infty). 
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
Note that, for the purposes of this work (cf. Theorem \ref{Thm2Intro}), the most interesting case concerns the fractional regime $\beta \in (0, 1)$. In sharp contrast to the  case $\beta = 0, 1$ exhibited in \eqref{2.9.3}, the spaces $\dot{B}^{\beta}_{q}(\gamma,\infty), \beta \in (0, 1),$ are not part of  classical scales of function spaces  such as fractional Sobolev spaces, Besov spaces or Triebel--Lizorkin spaces, but they constitute an independent scale; cf. \cite{DSSVSY}.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
The borderline case $p=2$ in Theorem \ref{Thm2Intro}  refers (at least,
formally) to  the Besov setting $f \in \dot{B}^\alpha_{2, \infty}$, cf. \eqref{2.9.4}.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- problem-1: body_md -->
\label{core_problem} Is it possible in general to characterize De Giorgi $ 
\Gamma$-convergence through a parameterized penalty method? Specifically,
does there exist a dynamic variant of the $K$-functional capable of
recovering $\Gamma$-limits while simultaneously providing a mechanism to
extract explicit rates of convergence for minimizers?
<!-- end problem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{teoarriba}
Let $\{F_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ be a sequence of
functionals on $X$. Then  for all $f\in X$,
\begin{align*}
\sup_{t>0}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(t,f;X,F_{n})}{t} & \leq
\Gamma_X-\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{n}(f) \\
& \hspace{-3cm}\leq\Gamma_X-\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{n}(f)\leq
\inf_{\{t_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty} \frac{K(t_n,f;X,F_{n})}{t_n},
\end{align*}
where the infimum runs over all infinitesimals $\{t_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \subset (0, \infty)$.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{ThmExtrapolIntro}
Let $p \in [1, \infty]$. If $f \in A_0$ then\footnote{As usual, we adopt the convention that the formula \eqref{9.9.4} for $f \in A_0 \backslash G_1(A_0, A_1)$ reads as $\Gamma_{A_0}-\lim_{\theta\to 1^-} \left\Vert f\right\Vert _{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta
,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}} = + \infty$.} 
\begin{equation}\label{9.9.4}
\Gamma_{A_0}-\lim_{\theta\rightarrow1^{-}}\Vert
f\Vert_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}=\left\Vert
f\right\Vert _{G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})}. 
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Consider the 
family of functionals $F_\theta, \theta \in (0, 1)$, defined on $A_0$ given by 
\begin{equation}\label{9.9.1}
F_{\theta}(f):= \begin{cases}
 \|f\|_{(A_0, A_1)_{\theta, p}^\blacktriangleleft} & \text{if} \qquad  f\in A_0 \cap (A_0, A_1)_{\theta, p}, \\ 
+\infty & \text{if} \qquad  f \in A_0 \backslash (A_0, A_1)_{\theta, p}.  
\end{cases} 
\end{equation}
  To implement the variational $K$-functional method we
need two elementary inequalities (see the proof of Theorem \ref{Thm1}  
below for full details): If $f \in A_0$ then 
\begin{equation}
K(t^{1/\theta},f;A_{0},A_{1})\leq K(t,f;A_{0},F_{\theta}), \qquad \forall t > 0, 
\label{elmal}
\end{equation}
and 
\begin{equation}
\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}nK\Big(\frac{1}{n},f; A_{0},F_{\theta_{n}} \Big) \leq\left 
\Vert f\right\Vert _{G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})},   \label{elbien}
\end{equation}
where $\theta_n \to 1^{-}$. 
It readily follows that 
\begin{align*}
	\|f\|_{G_1(A_0, A_1)} &= \sup_{t \in (0, \infty)} \frac{K(t, f; A_0, A_1)}{t} \\
	& = \sup_{t \in (0, \infty)} \liminf_{\theta \to 1^-}  \frac{K(t^{\frac{1}{\theta}}, f; A_0, A_1)}{t^{\frac{1}{\theta}}} \\
	& \leq \sup_{t \in (0, \infty)} \bigg( \liminf_{\theta \to 1^-} \frac{K(t, f; A_0, F_\theta)}{t} \bigg) \bigg(\liminf_{\theta \to 1^-} t^{1-\frac{1}{\theta}} \bigg) \qquad \text{(by \eqref{elmal})} \\
	& \leq \Gamma_{A_0}-\liminf_{\theta \to 1^-} F_\theta(f) \qquad \text{(by Theorem \ref{teoarriba})} \\
	& \leq \Gamma_{A_0}-\limsup_{\theta \to 1^-} F_\theta(f) \\
	& \leq \limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}nK\Big(\frac{1}{n}; f,A_{0},F_{\theta_{n}} \Big) \qquad \text{\Big(by Theorem \ref{teoarriba} with $t_n = \frac{1}{n}$ \Big)}   \\
	& \leq \|f\|_{G_1(A_0, A_1)} \qquad \text{(by \eqref{elbien})}. 
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{ThmCwiGen}
Suppose that $(A_0, A_1)$ is a pair of Banach spaces such that\footnote{The symbol $\hookrightarrow$ stands for the continuous embedding.}  
\begin{equation*}
	A_1 \hookrightarrow A_0 \qquad \text{is compact}.
\end{equation*}
Let $p\in\lbrack1,\infty
],\{\theta_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}\subset(0,1)$ with $\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\theta_{n}=1$,
and let $\{u_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ be a sequence such that 
\begin{equation*}
\liminf_{n\to \infty}\left\Vert u_{n}\right\Vert
_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta_{n},p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}<\infty. 
\end{equation*}
Then there exists $u\in A_{0}$ such that $ 
u_{n}\rightarrow u$ in $A_{0}$ as $n\rightarrow\infty$ (up to a subsequence).
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{ThmVarIntro}
Let $B$ and $A_0$ be Banach function spaces over a common finite measure space such that $B \hookrightarrow A_0$ and let $A_1$ be a Banach space satisfying  
\begin{equation}\label{23.9.1}
	A_0 \cap A_1 \hookrightarrow A_0 \qquad \text{is compact}.
\end{equation}
In addition, we assume that $B$ is uniformly convex. For each $f \in B, q, \lambda > 0,  p \in [1, \infty), \theta \in (0, 1)$, consider the interpolated functional given by
$$
	E_\theta (u):= \|f-u\|^q_B + \lambda \|u\|_{(A_0, A_1)_{\theta, p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}.  
$$
	If $\bar{u}_\theta$ are near-minimizers of $E_\theta$ then $\bar{u}_\theta \to \bar{u}$ in $B$ as $\theta \to 1^{-}$ (up to a subfamily), where $\bar{u}$ is a minimizer  of the Gagliardo functional
	\begin{equation}\label{23.9.4}
			E_1 (u):= \|f-u\|^q_B + \lambda \|u\|_{G_1(A_0, A_1)}.  
	\end{equation}
	Moreover
	\begin{equation}\label{20.9.9}
		E_1(\bar{u}) = \lim_{\theta \to 1^{-}} E_\theta(\bar{u}_\theta). 
	\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\begin{enumerate}
		\item[(i)] In view of applications to image processing (cf. Section \ref{Subsection1.11}  below), the assumption $B \hookrightarrow A_0$ seems to be quite natural.  Note that $A_1$ is  not necessarily a Banach function space in the sense of \cite{BS}. In particular, Sobolev spaces or more generally Besov--Triebel--Lizorkin spaces are admissible. 
		\item[(ii)] The uniform convexity of $B$  may be weakened to include non-uniformly convex spaces such as $L^1$. On the other hand, the fact that $B$ is uniformly
convex seems to be general enough to cover many classical spaces such as the 
$L^{p}$ spaces with $1<p<\infty$, Hilbert spaces, Lorentz spaces $ 
\Lambda^{p}(w)$ with $1<p<\infty$ and a regular weight $w$ (cf. \cite{H} for
further details), suitable Orlicz--Lorentz spaces, ....
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:lim}Let $p \in [1, \infty]$ and $i\in\{0,1\}$. Setting $i^{\ast
}=1-i$, we have
\begin{equation}
\lim_{\theta\rightarrow i}\left\Vert f\right\Vert _{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta
,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}=\left\Vert f\right\Vert _{G_{i}(A_{0},A_{1})}\qquad 
\text{for} \qquad f\in A_{i^{\ast}}.   \label{sec2:7}
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Consider the case $i=1$. For $f\in A_{0}\cap A_{1}$ the formula   
\eqref{sec2:7} was already established in \cite[Theorem 1]{M-Lim} (if $p < \infty$) and \cite[Lemma 1]{KMX} (if $p \leq \infty$), see also \cite{DM}. Applying this to the Gagliardo  pair $ 
\left( G_{0}(A_{0},A_{1}),G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})\right) $ and invoking  
\eqref{sec2:6}, we obtain 
\begin{equation}
\lim_{\theta\rightarrow1^{-}}\left\Vert f\right\Vert
_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}=\left\Vert f\right\Vert
_{G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})}, \qquad f\in G_{0}(A_{0},A_{1})\cap
G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1}).   \label{sec2:8}
\end{equation}
By the inclusion properties (\ref{sec:sera}), \eqref{sec2:8} also holds for $f\in
A_{0}\cap G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})$. It remains to check the validity of \eqref{sec2:8} for  $f\in A_{0}\setminus G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})
$. For this purpose, we note that \eqref{sec2:1} yields 
\begin{equation*}
\left\Vert f\right\Vert
_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}\geq\left\Vert f\right\Vert
_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,\infty}} \geq t^{-\theta} K(t, f; A_0, A_1) 
\end{equation*}
for every $t > 0$ and taking limits as $\theta\rightarrow1^{-}$:  
\begin{equation*}
\liminf_{\theta\rightarrow1^{-}}\left\Vert f\right\Vert
_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}\geq\frac{K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1}) 
}{t},\qquad\forall t>0. 
\end{equation*}
Taking now the supremum over $t>0$, we find that  
\begin{equation*}
\liminf_{\theta\rightarrow1^{-}}\left\Vert f\right\Vert
_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}\geq\left\Vert f\right\Vert
_{G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})} = + \infty,
\end{equation*}
which gives \eqref{sec2:7} for $i=1$ and $f  \not \in G_1(A_0, A_1)$.  

The case $i=0$ in  \eqref{sec2:7}  follows from the case $i=1$ when applied to the reversed pair $(A_{1},A_{0})$.
Indeed, since $K(t,f;A_{1},A_{0})=tK(\frac{1}{t},f;A_{0},A_{1})$, we have $\|f\|_{
G_{0}(A_{1},A_{0})}=\|f\|_{G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})}$ and $\|f\|_{(A_{1},A_{0})_{\theta,p}^{ 
\blacktriangleleft}}=\|f\|_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{1-\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}$.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{GdG} Let $\{F_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ and $\{G_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$
be sequences of functionals mapping a normed  space $X$ to $[0, + \infty]$. For each $f\in X$, the upper and lower generalized $ 
\Gamma $-limits are defined by 
\begin{equation*}
\Gamma-\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}(F_{n},G_{n})(f):=\inf\Big\{\limsup
_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{n}(f_{n}):\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset X \text{
with } \lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}G_{n}(f-f_n)=0\Big\}, 
\end{equation*}
\begin{equation*}
\Gamma-\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}(F_{n},G_{n})(f):=\inf\Big\{\liminf
_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{n}(f_{n}):\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset X \text{
with } \lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}G_{n}(f-f_n)=0\Big\}. 
\end{equation*}
The pair $(F_{n},G_{n})$ is said to $\Gamma$-converge if these limits
coincide for all $f\in X$, denoted by the common functional $ 
\Gamma-\lim_{n\rightarrow \infty}(F_{n},G_{n})$.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
This definition extends
analogously to families of functionals $\{F_{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}$ and $ 
\{G_{\varepsilon }\}_{\varepsilon>0}$.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{Remark3.2} If $G_{n}(f)=\Vert f\Vert_{X}$ for every $n\in\mathbb{N}$
and  $f\in X$, Definition \ref{GdG} reduces to classical $\Gamma $ 
-convergence  (cf. \eqref{intro1}-\eqref{intro2}) on $X$: 
\begin{equation*}
\Gamma-\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}(F_{n},G_{n})=\Gamma_{X}-\lim_{n\rightarrow
\infty}F_{n}. 
\end{equation*}
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{DefGKFunct} Given two functionals $F,G:X\rightarrow [0, + \infty]$, the variational  $K$-functional associated with the
pair $(G,F)$ is defined for each $f\in X$ and $t>0$ by 
\begin{equation*}
K(t,f;G,F):=\inf_{f=g+h}\{G(g)+tF(h)\}. 
\end{equation*}
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
Let $\text{\textrm{dom}}\, F = \{f\in X : F(f)<+\infty\}$. If $F$ and $G$
are norms, Definition \ref{DefGKFunct} coincides with the classical
$K$-functional (cf. \eqref{eq:classical_K_intro}), meaning $K(t,f;G,F) = K(t,f;\text{ 
\textrm{dom}}\,G, \text{\textrm{dom}}\,F)$ for $f \in\text{\textrm{dom}}\,G
+ \text{\textrm{dom}}\,F$. Then Definition \ref{DefGKFunct} yields the
natural non-linear (in the sense that norms are replaced by general functionals) extension of $K$-functionals.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{Thm3} Let $\{F_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ and $\{G_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
$ be sequences of functionals on $X$. Then, for any $f\in X$, 
\begin{align*}
\sup_{t>0}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(t,f;G_{n},F_{n})}{t} &
\leq\Gamma-\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}(F_{n},G_{n})(f) \\
& \leq\Gamma-\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}(F_{n},G_{n})(f)\leq \inf_{\{t_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}}\limsup
_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(t_n,f;G_{n},F_{n})}{t_n},
\end{align*}
where the infimum runs over all sequences $\{t_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \subset (0, \infty)$ such that $t_n \to 0$ as $n \to \infty$.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
We first prove the upper bound, more precisely, given any sequence $\{t_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ formed by positive numbers with  $t_n \to 0$ as $n \to \infty$ then 
\begin{equation}
\Gamma-\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}(F_{n},G_{n})(f)\leq  \limsup
_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(t_n,f;G_{n},F_{n})}{t_n}.   \label{P3.122}
\end{equation}
Suppose that $L:=\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}K\big(t_n,f;G_{n},F_{n} 
\big)/t_n<  \infty$, otherwise \eqref{P3.122} holds trivially. In particular,  $K(t_n, f; G_n, F_n) < +\infty$ for all sufficiently large $n \geq n_0$. Given  $n\geq n_0$, choose a decomposition $f=g_{n}+h_{n}$ satisfying 
\begin{equation}
G_{n}(g_{n})+t_n F_{n}(h_{n})\leq K(t_n,f;G_{n},F_{n}) 
+t_n^2.   \label{P2.1_thm}
\end{equation}
This implies that 
\begin{equation*}
G_{n}(f-h_{n})=G_{n}(g_{n})\leq K(t_n,f;G_{n},F_{n})+t^2_n. 
\end{equation*}
Taking limits yields 
\begin{equation*}
\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}G_{n}(f-h_{n})\leq\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty }
 \frac{K (t_n,f;G_{n},F_{n})}{t_n} t_n =L 
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty} t_n =0, 
\end{equation*}
which confirms that $G_{n}(f-h_{n})\rightarrow0$ as $n\rightarrow\infty$.
Moreover, from \eqref{P2.1_thm} we have 
\begin{equation*}
F_{n}(h_{n})\leq \frac{K(t_n,f;G_{n},F_{n})}{t_n}+t_n. 
\end{equation*}
Taking now limits on both sides of the previous estimate  we obtain $\limsup_{n\rightarrow 
\infty}F_{n}(h_{n})\leq L$. By Definition \ref{GdG}, this implies $\Gamma
-\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}(F_{n},G_{n})(f)\leq L$, and \eqref{P3.122}
follows.

Next we establish the lower bound: 
\begin{equation}
\sup_{t>0}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(t,f;G_{n},F_{n})}{t}\leq
\Gamma-\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}(F_{n},G_{n})(f).   \label{P3.322}
\end{equation}
Let $M:=\Gamma-\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}(F_{n},G_{n})(f)$. We can assume $ 
M<\infty$. Given $\varepsilon>0$, choose a sequence $\{f_{n}\}_{n\in 
\mathbb{N}} \subset X$ such that $G_{n}(f-f_n)\rightarrow0$ as $n\rightarrow \infty$
and 
\begin{equation}
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{n}(f_{n})\leq M+\varepsilon. 
\label{P2.2_thm}
\end{equation}
Fix $t>0$. There exists $m_0 = m_{0}(t,\varepsilon)\in\mathbb{N}$ such
that 
\begin{equation}
G_{n}(f-f_{n})\leq\varepsilon t\qquad\text{for all} \qquad n\geq
m_{0}.   \label{intermedia}
\end{equation}
Let $n\geq m_{0}$. To compute $K(t,f;G_{n},F_{n})$ consider
the decomposition $f=(f-f_{n})+f_{n}$, and use (\ref{intermedia}) to obtain 
\begin{equation}
\frac{K(t,f;G_{n},F_{n})}{t}\leq\frac{G_{n}(f-f_{n})}{t}+F_{n}(f_{n})\leq 
\varepsilon+F_{n}(f_{n}).   \label{P2.3_thm}
\end{equation}
Taking limits as $n\rightarrow\infty$ on both sides of  
\eqref{P2.3_thm} and applying \eqref{P2.2_thm}, 
\begin{equation*}
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(t,f;G_{n},F_{n})}{t}\leq\varepsilon
+\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{n}(f_{n})\leq2\varepsilon+M. 
\end{equation*}
Since this holds for any arbitrarily chosen $t>0$, we take the supremum over 
$t>0$ to arrive at: 
\begin{equation*}
\sup_{t>0}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(t,f;G_{n},F_{n})}{t} 
\leq2\varepsilon+M. 
\end{equation*}
Since $\varepsilon>0$ was arbitrary, passing to the limit as $\varepsilon
\rightarrow0^{+}$ establishes the desired estimate \eqref{P3.322}.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{Thm1} Let $p\in\lbrack1,\infty], i\in\{0,1\}$ and set $i^{\ast}=1-i$. 
Then for $f\in A_{i}$ we have 
\begin{equation}\label{9.9.2}
\Gamma_{A_{i}}-\lim_{\theta\rightarrow i^{\ast}}\Vert
f\Vert_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}=\Vert
f\Vert_{G_{i^{\ast}}(A_{0},A_{1})}. 
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
The proof for  the case $i=0$ was already given in Theorem \ref{ThmExtrapolIntro}, modulo the validity of the estimates \eqref{elmal} and \eqref{elbien}. Let us first check \eqref{elmal}: For every $t>0$ and  any decomposition $f = f_0 + f_1$ with $f_0 \in A_0$ and $f_1 \in(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}$,
we have 
\begin{align}
K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1}) & \leq K(t,f_0;A_{0},A_{1})+K(t,f_1; A_{0},A_{1})   \leq\Vert f_0\Vert_{A_{0}}+K(t,f_1;A_{0},A_{1}).   \label{Al1}
\end{align}
Moreover, by virtue of \eqref{sec2:1} it follows that 
\begin{equation*}
K(t,f_1;A_{0},A_{1})\leq t^{\theta}\Vert f_1\Vert_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta
,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}},\qquad\forall t>0. 
\end{equation*}
Then updating the estimate \eqref{Al1}, we obtain 
\begin{equation}\label{9.9.3}
K(t^{1/\theta},f;A_{0},A_{1})\leq\Vert f_0\Vert_{A_{0}}+t\Vert f_1\Vert
_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}} = \|f_0\|_{A_0} + t F_\theta(f_1),
\end{equation}
where  $F_{\theta}$ is the family of functionals given by \eqref{9.9.1}. 
Moreover, this estimate holds trivially if $f_1 \in A_0 \backslash (A_0, A_1)_{\theta, p}$ (since $F_\theta(f_1) = +\infty$). Then taking the infimum in \eqref{9.9.3} over all $f = f_0 + f_1$ with $f_0, f_1 \in A_0$, we arrive
at 
\begin{equation*}
K(t^{1/\theta},f;A_{0},A_{1})\leq K(t,f;A_{0},F_{\theta}).  
\end{equation*}
This proves \eqref{elmal}. 

Next we turn our attention to  \eqref{elbien}. By the definition of $K$-functional and applying Proposition \ref{prop:lim}, we get
\begin{align*}
	\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}nK\Big(\frac{1}{n},f,A_{0},F_{\theta_{n}} \Big) \leq \limsup_{n \to \infty} F_{\theta_n}(f) = \|f\|_{G_1(A_0, A_1)}. 
\end{align*}
This completes the proof of \eqref{9.9.2} with $i=0$. 
 
                                 
 
                                                                           
                                             
                 
                  
                  
                                                                    
                                 
                            
            
                        
               
                                                                      
                         
              
                                                                  
                                                                     
                                       
                                                                              
                        
                                                                              
                
            
                                                                      
 
                                                                             
                                                                           
                                                      
                  
                                                            
                
                                           
               
                                                                            
                                                
                                              
                                         
                                                                       
                                                            
                                                                   
                                                                            
                                                                     
             
                                                                           
                  
                                                                    
                        
                
 
                                 
 
                                                                            
          
               
                                                                              
                                                                
             
                                                        
                   
                                                                       
                                               
                 
                                               
                   
                                                                       
                                                  
                 
                                                                             
     
                  
                                                                           
                
                                                                             
                   
                                                                              
                 
                  
               
                                                                            
   
                                                                               
     
                                                                          
                          
              
                 
                                                                    
                                             
               
 
                                                          
                  
      
                                                                         
                

The case $i=1$ can be obtained from \eqref{9.9.2} with $i=0$ when applied to the reverse pair $(A_1, A_0)$. Indeed, in this case we have
\begin{equation}\label{14.9.1}
\Gamma_{A_{1}}-\lim_{\theta\rightarrow1^{-}}\Vert
f\Vert_{(A_{1},A_{0})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}=\Vert
f\Vert_{G_{1}(A_{1},A_{0})},\qquad f\in A_{1}. 
\end{equation}
Now since $K(t,f;A_{1},A_{0})=tK(\frac{1}{t},f;A_{0},A_{1}),$ we get
\begin{align}\label{14.9.2}
\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(A_{1},A_{0})} & =\sup_{t > 0}\frac{K(t,f;A_{1},A_{0})}{t}  =\sup_{t > 0}K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1})  =\Vert f\Vert_{G_{0}(A_{0},A_{1})}
\end{align}
and, taking into account that $c_{\theta, p} = c_{1-\theta, p}$, 
\begin{align}
	\Vert
f\Vert_{(A_{1},A_{0})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}} &= c_{\theta, p} \bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty
}[t^{-\theta}K(t,f;A_{1},A_{0})]^{p}\,\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/p} \nonumber \\
& =  c_{\theta, p} \bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty
}[t^{1-\theta}K(t^{-1},f;A_{0},A_{1})]^{p}\,\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/p} \nonumber  \\
& = c_{1-\theta, p} \|f\|_{(A_0, A_1)_{1-\theta, p}} = \Vert
f\Vert_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{1-\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}.  \label{14.9.3} 
\end{align}
Here, we assume that $p < \infty$, while the case $p=\infty$ is even easier. 
As a combination of \eqref{14.9.1}, \eqref{14.9.2} and \eqref{14.9.3}, 
$$
	\Gamma_{A_1}-\lim_{\theta \to 0^{+}} \Vert
f\Vert_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}} = \|f\|_{G_0(A_0, A_1)}, \qquad f \in A_1. 
$$
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- theorem-9: body_md -->
\label{ThmGKFunct} We have
\begin{equation}
\Gamma_{A_{0}}-\lim_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1})}{t}=\Vert
f\Vert_{G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})}  \qquad \text{if} \qquad f \in A_0  \label{PJ22.2}
\end{equation}
and 
\begin{equation}
\Gamma_{A_{1}}-\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1})=\Vert f\Vert
_{G_{0}(A_{0},A_{1})} \qquad \text{if} \qquad f \in A_1.   \label{PJ22.3}
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-9 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
We show \eqref{PJ22.2}. For each $n\in{\mathbb{N}}$, consider 
\begin{equation}\label{1.10.6}
F_{n}(f):=nK\Big(\frac{1}{n},f;A_{0},A_{1}\Big), \qquad f \in A_0. 
\end{equation}
On the one hand, we have the trivial estimate 
\begin{equation}
nK\Big(\frac{1}{n},f;A_{0},F_{n}\Big)\leq F_{n}(f).   \label{PJ4.2}
\end{equation}
On the other hand, given any decomposition $f = f_0 + f_1$ with $f_0, f_1 \in A_0$  and $t>0$, we have 
\begin{align}
K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1}) & \leq K(t,f_0;A_{0},A_{1})+K(t,f_1;A_{0},A_{1})  \leq\Vert f_0\Vert_{A_{0}}+K(t,f_1;A_{0},A_{1}).   \label{PJ4.1}
\end{align}
Without loss of generality, we may assume that $n\in{\mathbb{N}}$ is
sufficiently large so that $\frac{1}{n}\leq t$ and therefore (by basic monotonicity properties of $K$-functionals) 
\begin{equation*}
K(t,f_1;A_{0},A_{1})\leq tnK\Big(\frac{1}{n},f_1;A_{0},A_{1}\Big). 
\end{equation*}
Inserting this into \eqref{PJ4.1}, we arrive at 
\begin{align*}
K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1}) & \leq\Vert f_0\Vert_{A_{0}}+tnK\Big(\frac{1}{n} 
,f_1;A_{0},A_{1}\Big)  =\Vert f_0\Vert_{A_{0}}+tF_{n}(f_1).
\end{align*}
Taking the infimum over all possible decompositions $f = f_0 + f_1$, we obtain 
\begin{equation*}
K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1})\leq K(t,f;A_{0},F_{n}) 
\end{equation*}
if $\frac{1}{n}\leq t$. In particular 
\begin{equation}
K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1})\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}K(t,f;A_{0},F_{n}). 
\label{PJ4.3}
\end{equation}

Applying  now Theorem \ref{teoarriba} to the functionals $F_n$ given by \eqref{1.10.6} defined on $X=A_{0}$, combined with \eqref{PJ4.2}
and \eqref{PJ4.3}, gives 
\begin{align*}
\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})} & =\sup_{t>0}\frac{K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1})}{t}
 \leq \sup_{t > 0}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(t,f;A_{0},F_{n})}{t} \\
& \leq\Gamma_{A_{0}}-\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{n}(f)  \leq\Gamma_{A_{0}}-\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{n}(f) \\
& \leq\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}nK\Big(\frac{1}{n},f;A_{0},F_{n}\Big)  \leq\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{n}(f) \leq \Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})}. 
\end{align*}
As a byproduct, we have shown that 
\begin{equation*}
\Gamma_{A_{0}}-\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}F_{n}(f)=\Vert
f\Vert_{G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})} \qquad \text{if} \qquad f \in A_0. 
\end{equation*}
This completes the proof of \eqref{PJ22.2}.

The assertion \eqref{PJ22.3} is an immediate consequence of \eqref{PJ22.2}
applied to the reverse pair $(A_{1},A_{0})$.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- theorem-10: body_md -->
\label{Thm54} Let $q\in\lbrack1,\infty]$ and $k\in{\mathbb{N}}$. Then for $ 
f\in W^k X$ we have 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{k}X} \lesssim \Gamma_{X}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B} 
_{q}^{sk}X}\leq \Gamma_{X}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot {B}_{q}^{sk}X}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{k}X}. 
\end{equation*}
<!-- end theorem-10 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
In particular, applying Theorem \ref{Thm54} with $X=L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),k=1
$ and $q=p,$ we obtain (cf. \eqref{CoincGFS}) 
\begin{align*}
C_1 \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}& \leq \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/p}\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})} \\
& \leq \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/p}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{s,p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq C_2 \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}, 
\end{align*}
which gives the $L^p$-topology analog of  \eqref{S5.1}, up to equivalence constants (cf. Section \ref{SectionExact} below for a detailed study on exact equivalence constants). More generally, if $k \in \mathbb{N}$ then
\begin{align}
C_1 \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}& \leq \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/p}\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{B}^{s k}_{p, p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})} \nonumber\\
& \leq \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/p}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B}^{s k}_{p, p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq C_2 \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}.  \label{14.9.8}
\end{align}
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Apply Theorem \ref{Thm1} to the pair $(A_{0},A_{1})=(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)$, then 
\begin{equation}
\Gamma_{X}-\lim_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}\Vert f\Vert_{(X,\dot{W} 
^{k}X)^{\blacktriangleleft}_{s,q}}=\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)}   \label{PD28.2}
\end{equation}
for every $f\in X$. The method of proof now consists in estimating
appropriately both sides of \eqref{PD28.2}.

Now, for each $t>0$ and $f \in X$,  
\begin{equation}
K(t^{k},f;X,\dot{W}^{k}X)\approx  \begin{cases}
\big(\frac{1}{t^{N}}\int_{|h|<t}\Vert\Delta_{h}^{k}f\Vert_{X}^{q}\,dh\big) 
^{1/q} & \qquad \text{if} \qquad q \in (0, \infty), \\
\omega_k(f, t)_X & \qquad \text{if} \qquad q = \infty,   \label{PD28.1}
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}\label{15.9.1}
\omega_k(f, t)_X:=	\sup_{|h| \leq t} \|\Delta^k_h f\|_X
\end{equation}
is the classical \emph{$k$-th order modulus of smoothness} of $f$. For $X=L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}), \, p \in [1, \infty]$, the formula \eqref{PD28.1} with $q=\infty$ is well known (see e.g. \cite[Theorem 4.12, p. 339, (4.42), p. 341]{BS}), while the case $q < \infty$ may be found
in \cite[Appendix A]{KMX}; in this case, we shall use the simplified notation $\omega_k(f, t)_p$ to denote $\omega_k(f, t)_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)}$ and, when there is no danger of confusion, $\omega(f, t)_p := \omega_1(f, t)_p$. For general r.i. spaces,  the proof of \eqref{PD28.1} follows along similar
lines as in the setting of Lebesgue spaces, cf. \cite[Lemma B.1]{DLTYY} for further details.

Let $s\in(0,1)$ and $q \in [1, \infty)$. Using successively the definition of the
interpolation norm, a change of variables,  the characterization of the $K$-functional \eqref{PD28.1} 
and Fubini's theorem, we get 
\begin{align}
\Vert f\Vert_{(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)_{s,q}} & \approx\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty
}t^{-skq}K(t^{k},f;X,\dot{W}^{k}X)^{q}\,\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}  \notag \\
& \approx\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-skq-N}\int_{|h|<t}\Vert\Delta_{h}^{k}f 
\Vert_{X}^{q}\,dh\,\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}  \notag \\
& \approx\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{\Vert\Delta_{h}^{k}f\Vert_{X}^{q}}{ 
|h|^{skq+N}}\,dh\bigg)^{1/q}=\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B}_{q}^{sk}X}. 
\label{PD28.3}
\end{align}
Here, we stress that all underlying equivalence constants are uniform with respect to $s \in (0, 1)$.  The
case $q=\infty$ in \eqref{PD28.3} is even easier and then we shall omit the
details.

We estimate the left-hand side of \eqref{PD28.2} via \eqref{PD28.3}, 
\begin{align*}
\Gamma_{X}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}\Vert f\Vert_{(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)_{s,q}^\blacktriangleleft}
& \lesssim\Gamma_{X}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot {B 
}_{q}^{sk}X}   \\
& \leq\Gamma_{X}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B} 
_{q}^{sk}X}   \\
& \lesssim\Gamma_{X}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}\Vert f\Vert_{(X,\dot{W} 
^{k}X)_{s,q}^\blacktriangleleft}.   
\end{align*}
Using these estimates in \eqref{PD28.2}, we achieve that, for $f \in X$, 
\begin{align}
	\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)}  &\lesssim \Gamma_{X}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot {B 
}_{q}^{sk}X}  \notag \\
& \leq\Gamma_{X}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B} 
_{q}^{sk}X}  \notag \\
& \lesssim \Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)}. \label{13.9.1}
\end{align}

To estimate $\|f\|_{G_1(X, \dot{W}^k X)}$, we will make use of a result obtained in \cite[(6.8)]{DLTYY}: If $f\in\dot{W}^{k}X$ and $q \in (0, \infty)$ then the following limit exists  
\begin{equation*}
	\lim_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{\big(\frac{1}{t^{N/k}}\int_{|h|<t^{1/k}} 
\Vert\Delta_{h}^{k}f\Vert_{X}^{q}\,dh\big)^{1/q}}{t} < \infty
\end{equation*}
and, in addition,  its value (which can be explicitly computed) satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{10.9.2}
	\lim_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{\big(\frac{1}{t^{N/k}}\int_{|h|<t^{1/k}} 
\Vert\Delta_{h}^{k}f\Vert_{X}^{q}\,dh\big)^{1/q}}{t}  \approx \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{k}X}. 
\end{equation}
Concerning the case $q= \infty$, we make use of the fact that (see \cite[(1.8)]{T} for the case $X = L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)$ but the arguments can be easily adapted to work with any r.i. space)
\begin{equation}\label{12.9.1}
	\lim_{t \to 0} \frac{\omega_k(f, t)_X}{t^k} = \sup_{h \neq 0} \frac{\|\Delta^k_h f\|_X}{|h|^k}
\end{equation}
provided that $f \in \dot{W}^k X$. Note that the right-hand side of \eqref{12.9.1} is finite due to the  fact that  (cf. \cite[(6.6)]{DLTYY}) 
$$
	 \sup_{h \neq 0} \frac{\|\Delta^k_h f\|_X}{|h|^k} \approx \|f\|_{\dot{W}^k X}, \qquad f \in \dot{W}^k X. 
$$
Hence the counterpart of \eqref{10.9.2} related to $q=\infty$ reads 
\begin{equation}\label{12.9.2}
	\lim_{t \to 0} \frac{\omega_k(f, t)_X}{t^k}  \approx \|f\|_{\dot{W}^k X}. 
\end{equation}



Assume that $f\in\dot{W}^{k}X$. Using \eqref{3.9.1}, \eqref{PD28.1} and \eqref{10.9.2}, we derive
\begin{align}
\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)} & =\lim_{t\rightarrow0}\frac {K(t,f;X, 
\dot{W}^{k}X)}{t}   \approx \lim_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{\big(\frac{1}{t^{N/k}}\int_{|h|<t^{1/k}} 
\Vert\Delta_{h}^{k}f\Vert_{X}^{q}\,dh\big)^{1/q}}{t}  \approx\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{k}X}   \label{PD28.5i}
\end{align}
if $q < \infty$. The case $q=\infty$ follows similarly but using instead \eqref{12.9.2}. 


Updating \eqref{13.9.1} with \eqref{PD28.5i}, we find for $f \in W^k X$
\begin{align*}
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{k}X} & \lesssim\Gamma_{X}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow
1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B}_{q}^{sk}X} \\
& \leq\Gamma_{X}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B} 
_{q}^{sk}X}\lesssim\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{k}X}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- theorem-11: body_md -->
\label{Thm14.9.26}
	Let $p \in (1, \infty)$ and $t > 0$. Then for $f \in H^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N)$ we have
	\begin{align}
	 \|f\|_{\dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N)} &\lesssim \Gamma_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)}-\liminf_{s \to 1^{-}} (1-s)^{1/p} \|f\|_{\dot{W}^{s, p}(\mathbb{R}^N), t} \nonumber \\
		&  \leq \Gamma_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)}-\limsup_{s \to 1^{-}} (1-s)^{1/p} \|f\|_{\dot{W}^{s, p}(\mathbb{R}^N), t}  \lesssim  \|f\|_{\dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N)}.  \label{14.9.9}
	\end{align}
<!-- end theorem-11 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)]	Let $t = k \in \mathbb{N}$ in Theorem \ref{Thm14.9.26}. Using the well-known fact that $$\dot{H}^{k, p}(\mathbb{R}^N) = \dot{W}^{k, p}(\mathbb{R}^N), \qquad p \in (1, \infty),$$
	and \eqref{14.9.7}, then \eqref{14.9.9} gives back \eqref{14.9.8}. 
\item[(ii)] 
	The pointwise counterpart of Theorem \ref{Thm14.9.26} was already obtained in \cite[Theorem 3.4]{DM}. 
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Consider the pair $(A_0, A_1) = (L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))$. Applying Theorem \ref{Thm1} we infer that 
	\begin{equation}\label{14.9.10}
		\Gamma_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)}-\lim_{s \to 1^{-}} \|f\|_{(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))_{s, p}^{\blacktriangleleft}} = \|f\|_{G_1(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))} 
	\end{equation}
	if $f \in L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)$. 
	
	Next we identify the corresponding interpolation norms and Gagliardo completions that arise in \eqref{14.9.10}.  To do this, we shall employ the following characterization of the $K$-functional relative to $(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))$ in terms of Butzer operators: For $u > 0$ and $f \in L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)$, 
	\begin{equation}\label{14.9.11}
		K(u^t, f; L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N)) \approx \frac{1}{u^N} \int_{|h| < u} \|\Delta^t_h f\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)} \, dh \approx \sup_{|h| < u} \|\Delta^t_h f\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)};
	\end{equation}
	cf. \cite[(1.14) and (1.35)]{KT}. 
	
	Assume that $s \in (0, 1)$. By a simple change of variables 
	\begin{align}\label{14.9.12}
		\|f\|_{(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))_{s, p}} & \approx  \bigg(\int_0^\infty [u^{-s t} K(u^t, f; L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N) )]^p \, \frac{du}{u} \bigg)^{1/p}
	\end{align} 
	and using the first estimate in \eqref{14.9.11} together with H\"older's inequality with exponent $p$ and Fubini's theorem,  we obtain that
	\begin{align}
		\|f\|_{(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))_{s, p}} & \approx   \bigg(\int_0^\infty \bigg[u^{-s t-N}  \int_{|h| < u} \|\Delta^t_h f\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)} \, dh \bigg]^p \, \frac{du}{u} \bigg)^{1/p} \nonumber \\
		& \lesssim  \bigg(\int_0^\infty u^{-s t p-N}  \int_{|h| < u} \|\Delta^t_h f\|^p_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)} \, dh \, \frac{du}{u} \bigg)^{1/p} \nonumber \\
		& \approx \bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^N}  |h|^{-s t p -N} \|\Delta^t_h f\|^p_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)}  \, dh \bigg)^{1/p} = \|f\|_{\dot{W}^{s, p}(\mathbb{R}^N), t}, \label{14.9.13}
	\end{align}
	where all equivalence constants may be taken to be independent of $s \in (0, 1)$. Conversely,  the second estimate in \eqref{14.9.11} gives 
	\begin{align*}
		  \|f\|_{\dot{W}^{s, p}(\mathbb{R}^N), t} &  \lesssim \bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^N}  |h|^{-s t p -N} K(|h|^t, f; L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))^p  \, dh \bigg)^{1/p}
	\end{align*}
	and then applying polar coordinates
	\begin{align}
		 \|f\|_{\dot{W}^{s, p}(\mathbb{R}^N), t} & \lesssim  \bigg(\int_0^\infty  u^{-s t p} K(u^t, f; L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))^p  \, \frac{du}{u} \bigg)^{1/p} \nonumber \\
		 & \approx \|f\|_{(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))_{s, p}}, \label{14.9.14}
	\end{align}
	where we have used \eqref{14.9.12} in the last step. Once again, we observe that all underlying equivalence constants in \eqref{14.9.14} are independent of $s$. Putting now together \eqref{14.9.13} and \eqref{14.9.14}, we have shown that 
	\begin{equation}\label{14.9.15}
		 \|f\|_{(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))_{s, p}} \approx  \|f\|_{\dot{W}^{s, p}(\mathbb{R}^N), t} 
	\end{equation}
	uniformly with respect to $s$. 
	
	Next we turn our attention to $G_1(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))$. It follows from \eqref{14.9.11} that 
	\begin{align}
		\|f\|_{G_1(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))} &= \sup_{u > 0} \frac{K(u^t, f; L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N)) }{u^t} \nonumber \\
		& \approx \sup_{h \neq 0}  \frac{\|\Delta^t_h f\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)}}{|h|^t}. \label{14.9.16}
	\end{align}
	Moreover, according to \cite[Lemma 3.6]{DT2}, if $f \in H^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N)$ then 
	$$
	\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N)}	\approx \sup_{h \neq 0}  \frac{\|\Delta^t_h f\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)}}{|h|^t}. 
	$$
	Hence \eqref{14.9.16} can be updated to say that 
	\begin{equation}\label{14.9.17}
		\|f\|_{G_1(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N))}  \approx \|f\|_{\dot{H}^{t, p}(\mathbb{R}^N)}. 
	\end{equation}
	
	From \eqref{14.9.10}, \eqref{14.9.15} and \eqref{14.9.17}, we infer that \eqref{14.9.9} holds.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- theorem-12: body_md -->
\label{ThmTV} Let $q\in\lbrack1,\infty]$ and $k\in{\mathbb{N}}$. Then, for $f \in BV^k (\mathbb{R}^N)$, we have 
\begin{align*}
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{BV}^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{N})} &\lesssim \Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{B}_{1,q}^{sk}(\mathbb{R}^{N})} \\
& \leq\Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B}_{1,q}^{sk}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{BV}^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}. 
\end{align*}
<!-- end theorem-12 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
From \eqref{13.9.1} applied with $X=L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$, it follows that for $f \in L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$, 
\begin{align*}
\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{k,1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}))} & \lesssim \Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{B}_{1,q}^{sk}(\mathbb{R}^{N})} \\
& \leq \Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow1^{-}}(1-s)^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot {B}_{1,q}^{sk}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})} \\
& \lesssim \Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{k,1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}))}.
\end{align*}
To conclude it remains to prove that 
\begin{equation}
\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{k,1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}))}\approx\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{\text{$BV$}}^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}, \qquad f \in \dot{BV}^k(\mathbb{R}^N). 
\label{PJ1.1}
\end{equation}

Let us compute $G_{1}(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{k,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))$.
Using \eqref{PD28.1}, 
\begin{align}
\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{k,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))} &
=\sup_{t > 0}\frac{K(t^{k},f;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{k,1}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t^{k}}     \approx\sup_{t > 0}\frac{\omega_{k}(f,t)_{1}}{t^{k}}.   \label{PJ1.2}
\end{align}
Here,  we use the abbreviated notation $\omega_k(f, t)_1 := \omega_k(f, t)_{L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)}$.  To prove \eqref{PJ1.1}
when $k=1$ we use \eqref{PJ1.2} and the well-known characterization (see e.g. \cite{CDDD}) of the
total variation in terms of the moduli of smoothness: 
\begin{equation}\label{13.9.2}
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{\text{$BV$}}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\approx \sup_{t > 0} \frac{\omega(f, t)_1}{t}.
\end{equation}
 It follows from \eqref{PJ1.2} that 
$$
	\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}  \approx \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{\text{$BV$}}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}
$$
and then \eqref{PJ1.1} with $k=1$ holds. For the case $k>1$ we use  the classical
estimates for the moduli of smoothness of derivatives  (cf. \cite[(12.2) and (12.3), p. 117] 
{DT}) 
\begin{equation}
t^{-(k-1)}\omega_{k}(f,t)_{1}\lesssim\sup_{|\alpha|=k-1}\omega_{1}(D^{\alpha
}f,t)_{1}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}u^{-(k-1)}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{1}\,\frac{du}{u}. 
\label{PJ1.4}
\end{equation}
Note that \eqref{PJ1.4} leads to 
\begin{equation*}
\sup_{t > 0}\frac{\omega_{k}(f,t)_{1}}{t^{k}}\approx\sup_{|\alpha
|=k-1}\sup_{t > 0}\frac{\omega_{1}(D^{\alpha}f,t)_{1}}{t}
\end{equation*}
and therefore, by \eqref{PJ1.2} and \eqref{13.9.2},  
\begin{align*}
\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{k,1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}))} & \approx\sup_{|\alpha|=k-1}\sup_{t > 0}\frac{\omega_{1}(D^{\alpha
}f,t)_{1}}{t} \\
& \approx\sup_{|\alpha|=k-1}\Vert D^{\alpha}f\Vert_{\dot {\text{$ 
BV$}}(\mathbb{R}^{N})} \approx \|f\|_{\dot{BV}^k(\mathbb{R}^N)}. 
\end{align*}
This proves the desired claim \eqref{PJ1.1} with $k > 1$ and completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- theorem-13: body_md -->
We have, for $f \in L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$,  
\begin{equation}
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot { 
BV}(\mathbb{R}^{N})} \lesssim \Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{\varepsilon\rightarrow0}\kappa_{ 
\varepsilon}(f)\leq \Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\limsup
_{\varepsilon\rightarrow0}\kappa_{\varepsilon}(f)\lesssim \Vert f\Vert_{\dot { 
BV}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}.   \label{ARBDN2}
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-13 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
To apply the extrapolation method we shall use the following characterization
of the $K$-functional for the pair $(L^{1},\dot{W}^{1,1})$, which has been recently obtained in \cite 
{DS}: for all $\varepsilon>0$ and $f \in L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$, 
\begin{equation}
\kappa_{c \varepsilon}(f) \lesssim \frac{K(\varepsilon,f;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}))}{\varepsilon} \lesssim \kappa_{c' \varepsilon}(f)   \label{K-Funct-DS}
\end{equation}
for some constants $c, c' > 0$. 
Accordingly, the validity of \eqref{ARBDN2} follows immediately from Theorem  
\ref{ThmGKFunct} specialised to the pair $(A_0, A_1) = (L^1(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{W}^{1, 1}(\mathbb{R}^N))$ and the formula \eqref{PJ1.1} with $k=1$ (which remains valid adequately interpreted for $f \in L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$).
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- theorem-14: body_md -->
\label{ThmGCSMMS} Let $p \in [1, \infty)$ and $q \in[1, \infty]$. Then, for $f \in L^p(\mathcal{X}) \cap KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{X})$,  
\begin{align} 
\|f\|_{KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{X})}& \lesssim \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathcal{X})}-\liminf_{s \to1^{-}} (1-s)^{1/q} \|f\|_{\dot{ 
\mathcal{B}}^{s}_{p, q}(\mathcal{X})} \nonumber \\
& \leq \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathcal{X} 
)}-\limsup_{s \to1^{-}} (1-s)^{1/q} \|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{B}}^{s}_{p, q}( 
\mathcal{X})} \lesssim \|f\|_{KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{X})}.  \label{PJ4.7}
\end{align}
In particular, if $p=q$ then (cf. \eqref{EquivBGMMS}) 
\begin{align}
 \|f\|_{KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{ 
X})}^{p} &\lesssim \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathcal{X})}-\liminf_{s \to1^{-}} (1-s) \int_{\mathcal{X}} \int_{ 
\mathcal{X}} \frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{d(x, y)^{s p} \mu(B(x, d(x, y)))} \, d
\mu(y) \, d \mu(x)   \notag \\
&  \leq \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathcal{X})}-\limsup_{s \to1^{-}} (1-s)
\int_{\mathcal{X}} \int_{\mathcal{X}} \frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{d(x, y)^{s p}
\mu(B(x, d(x, y)))} \, d \mu(y) \, d \mu(x) \lesssim \|f\|_{KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{ 
X})}^{p}.   \label{PJ4.8}
\end{align}
<!-- end theorem-14 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
The counterpart of \eqref{PJ4.8} associated to 
pointwise convergence may be found in \cite{Mu} and \cite{DiMS}.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
We apply Theorem \ref{Thm1} to the pair $(A_{0},A_{1})=(L^{p}(\mathcal{X} 
),KS^{1,p}(\mathcal{X}))$. It follows from \cite[Theorem 4.1]{GKS} that 
\begin{equation}
K(t,f;L^{p}(\mathcal{X}),KS^{1,p}(\mathcal{X}))\approx E_{p,t}(f). 
\label{PJ4.4}
\end{equation}
As a consequence, we have
\begin{equation}
\Vert f\Vert_{(L^{p}(\mathcal{X}),KS^{1,p}(\mathcal{X}))_{s,q}}\approx\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{\mathcal{B}}_{p,q}^{s}(\mathcal{X})},   \label{PJ4.5}
\end{equation}
with underlying equivalence constants independent of $s\in(0,1)$.
Furthermore, from \eqref{PJ4.4}, we also derive that 
\begin{equation}
\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(L^{p}(\mathcal{X}),KS^{1,p}(\mathcal{X}))}\approx\Vert
f\Vert_{KS^{1,p}(\mathcal{X})}.   \label{PJ4.6}
\end{equation}
Combining \eqref{PJ4.5} and \eqref{PJ4.6} with Theorem \ref{Thm1}, we arrive
at the desired estimates \eqref{PJ4.7}.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- theorem-15: body_md -->
\label{Thm4.10.1}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$. Then, for $f \in L^p(\mathcal{X})$,  
\begin{equation}  \label{PJ5.1}
\|f\|_{KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{X})} \lesssim \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathcal{X})}-\liminf_{t \to0} \frac{E_{p, t}(f)}{t}
\leq \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathcal{X})}-\limsup_{t \to0} \frac{E_{p, t}(f)}{t}
\lesssim \|f\|_{KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{X})}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-15 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
Note that existence of a $\Gamma$-convergent subfamily in \eqref{PJ5.1} is always guaranteed in light of \cite[Theorem 8.5]{DALM}. In particular, the functional $\mathcal{E}_p$ in \eqref{4.10.2} is well-defined and \eqref{4.10.3} holds.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Consider the pair $(A_{0}, A_{1}) = (L^{p}(\mathcal{X}), KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{X 
}))$ and apply Theorem \ref{ThmGKFunct}. Then \eqref{PJ5.1} follows
immediately from the characterization of the corresponding $K$-functional
given in \eqref{PJ4.4} and the Gagliardo completion computed in \eqref{PJ4.6} 
.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- theorem-16: body_md -->
\label{THMGammaMS}
Let $q\in\lbrack1,\infty]$ and $k\in{\mathbb{N}}$. Then for $f\in W^k X$ we have
\begin{equation}
 \Vert f\Vert_{X} \lesssim \Gamma_{\dot{W}^{k}X}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow0^{+}}s^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B} 
_{q}^{sk}X}\leq \Gamma_{\dot{W}^{k}X}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow0^{+}}s^{1/q}\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{B}_{q}^{sk}X}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert_{X}.   \label{PJ6.3}
\end{equation}
In particular, for $k=1,X=L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$ and $q=p\in\lbrack1,\infty)
$, 
\begin{equation}
\Vert f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})} \lesssim \Gamma_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{s\rightarrow0^{+}}s^{1/p}\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq \Gamma_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}-\limsup_{s\rightarrow0^{+}}s^{1/p}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{s,p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}.   \label{PJ6.4}
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-16 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
We apply Theorem \ref{Thm1} to the pair $(A_{0},A_{1})=(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)$.
Recall that (cf. \eqref{PD28.3}) 
\begin{equation}
\Vert f\Vert_{(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)_{s,q}}\approx\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B}_{q}^{sk}X} 
\label{PJ6.1}
\end{equation}
uniformly in $s\in(0,1)$. Moreover, according to  \cite[ 
pp. 230-231]{DLTYY} 
\begin{equation*}
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{t^{N}}\int_{|h|<t}\Vert\Delta_{h}^{k}f 
\Vert_{X}^{q}\,dh=C_{N}\lim_{|h|\rightarrow\infty}\Vert\Delta_{h}^{k}f 
\Vert_{X}^{q}\approx\Vert f\Vert_{X}^{q}
\end{equation*}
if $q < \infty$. In the case $q=\infty$, it is clear that (cf. \eqref{15.9.1})
$$
	\lim_{t \to \infty} \omega_k(f, t)_X = \sup_{h \in \mathbb{R}^N} \|\Delta^k_h f\|_X \approx \|f\|_X. 
$$
Consequently, combining with \eqref{PD28.1} we obtain 
\begin{equation}
\Vert f\Vert_{G_{0}(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)}=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}K(t^{k},f;X, 
\dot{W}^{k}X)\approx\Vert f\Vert_{X}.   \label{PJ6.2}
\end{equation}
An application of Theorem \ref{Thm1}, taking into account \eqref{PJ6.1} and  
\eqref{PJ6.2}, gives the desired assertion \eqref{PJ6.3}.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
The method of proof of Theorem \ref{THMGammaMS} admits some improvements. First, it is possible to update the mode of $\Gamma$-convergence  
from $\dot{W}^{k} X$ to $X$. Second, the result is valid for $f \in X$. These issues, together with exact $\Gamma$-formulae,  will be discussed in Section \ref{SectionExact}  below.
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- theorem-17: body_md -->
\label{ThmJ22.1} Let $f\in BV(\mathbb{R}^{N}),$ then 
\begin{align*}
\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}& \lesssim \Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}} 
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|)(x)\,dx & \\
& \leq \Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\limsup_{t\rightarrow 0} 
\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|)(x)\,dx\lesssim \Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end theorem-17 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Assume first that $f \geq 0$. By definition of $H_t$, we have  
\begin{equation}
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|)(x)\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\int_{ 
\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{t}(x-y)|f(y)-f(x)|\,dy\,dx.    \label{uba}
\end{equation}
It is convenient to rewrite this formula using superlevel sets. Indeed, let 
\begin{equation*}
E_{\tau}^{f}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:f(x)>\tau\},  \qquad \tau \in \mathbb{R}, 
\end{equation*}
and observe that 
\begin{equation}
|f(x)-f(y)|=\int_0^\infty |\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}(x)-\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau}^{f}}(y)|\,d\tau.   \label{uba1}
\end{equation}
Update (\ref{uba}) using (\ref{uba1}) and then apply Fubini's theorem to obtain 
\begin{align}
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|)(x)\,dx &  =\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R} 
^{N}}h_{t}(x-y)|\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}(x)-\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}(y)| 
\,dy\bigg)dx\,d\tau  \notag \\
& =\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}- 
\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}(x)|)(x)\,dx\,d\tau.   \label{PJ6.5}
\end{align}
Since $\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{t}(x)\,dx=1,t>0,$ one can easily verify that 
\begin{equation*}
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}-\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau}^{f}}(x)|)(x)\,dx=\Vert H_{t}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}-\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau }^{f}}\Vert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}
\end{equation*}
and inserting this into \eqref{PJ6.5}, we find
that 
\begin{equation*}
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|)(x)\,dx=\int_0^\infty \Vert H_{t} 
\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}-\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}\,d\tau. 
\end{equation*}
Hence, the desired $\Gamma$-limit can be reduced to 
\begin{equation}
\Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\lim_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\int_{ 
\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|)(x)\,dx=\Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}-\lim_{t\rightarrow0}\int_0^\infty \frac{\Vert H_{t}\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau }^{f}}-\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}}{ 
\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau.   \label{PJ6.6}
\end{equation}

Consider the Riesz potential  space given by 
\begin{equation*}
\dot{H}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}):=\{f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}):\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{H}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}:=\Vert\Delta f\Vert _{L^{1}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}<\infty\};
\end{equation*}
cf. \eqref{15.9.2}. 
It is clear that 
\begin{equation}
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{H}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\lesssim\Vert f\Vert_{ 
\dot{W}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}   \label{PJ6.7}
\end{equation}
although the converse is not  true. We will make use of the known
fact that (see e.g. \cite[p. 99]{DT}) 
\begin{equation}
K(t,f;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{H}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}))\approx\Vert H_{t}f-f\Vert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}    \label{PJ6.8}
\end{equation}
if $f \in L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$. 
Specifically, we use \eqref{PJ6.8}
for the function $\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}} \in L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$ in order to write 
\begin{equation}
\int_0^\infty \frac{\Vert H_{t}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}-\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau\approx \int_0^\infty \frac{K(t,\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{H}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau.   \label{PJ6.9}
\end{equation}
To estimate the right-hand side of \eqref{PJ6.6} we can use \eqref{PJ6.7}
and \eqref{PD28.1},  which lead to 
\begin{align}\label{PJ.14}
K(t,f;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{H}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})) &
\lesssim K(t,f;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})) \approx \omega_{2}(f,\sqrt{t})_{1}
\end{align}
and combined with the trivial estimate 
\begin{equation*}
\omega_{2}(f,t)_{1}\lesssim \omega_{1}(f,t)_{1}
\end{equation*}
we derive 
\begin{equation*}
K(t,f;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{H}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}))\lesssim\omega_{1}(f,\sqrt{t})_{1}
\end{equation*}
or equivalently (apply again \eqref{PD28.1}) 
\begin{equation}
K(t,f;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{H}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))\lesssim
K(\sqrt{t},f;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})). 
\label{PJ6.17}
\end{equation}
Hence, we can estimate \eqref{PJ6.9} as follows 
\begin{equation}
\int_0^\infty \frac{\Vert H_{t}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}-\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau\lesssim \int_0^\infty \frac{K(\sqrt{t},\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}), 
\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau.   \label{PJ6.18}
\end{equation}
As a consequence, using the trivial fact that $\Gamma-\limsup$ of any family of functionals is dominated by the corresponding pointwise $\limsup$, we have 
\begin{align}
\Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\limsup_{t\rightarrow0}\int_0^\infty \frac{ 
\Vert H_{t}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}-\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{L^{1}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau & \nonumber \\ 
& \hspace{-6cm}\lesssim\limsup_{t\rightarrow 0}\int_0^\infty \frac{K(\sqrt{t},\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}), 
\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau.   \label{PJ6.10}
\end{align}
Note that, by \eqref{PJ1.1}, 
\begin{equation*}
\sup_{t>0}\frac{K(t,f;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t 
}=\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}))}\approx\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}. 
\end{equation*}
Applying this to $\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}$, we have 
\begin{equation}
\sup_{t>0}\frac{K(t,\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot {W} 
^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t}\approx\Vert\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert _{\dot{ 
BV}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}   \label{PJ6.11}
\end{equation}
and, by the classical co-area formula for $BV$ (see e.g. 
\cite[Theorem 2.2]{CDDD}): 
\begin{equation}
\int_{0}^\infty \Vert\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{\dot{BV}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}\,d\tau=\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}<\infty. 
\label{PJ6.12}
\end{equation}
Accordingly, one can apply the reverse Fatou's lemma together with a simple change of
variables to get that 
\begin{align*}
\limsup_{t\rightarrow0}\int_0^\infty \frac{K(\sqrt{t},\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau
}^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{\sqrt{t}} 
\,d\tau & \leq\int_0^\infty \limsup_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{K(t,\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau }^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t} 
\,d\tau \\
& \hspace{-5cm}=\int_0^\infty \sup_{t>0}\frac{K(t,\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau}^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t} 
\,d\tau \approx\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{N})},
\end{align*}
where the last step follows from \eqref{PJ6.11}-\eqref{PJ6.12}. Plugging
this into \eqref{PJ6.10}, we arrive at 
\begin{equation}
\Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\limsup_{t\rightarrow0}\int_0^\infty \frac{ 
\Vert H_{t}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}-\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{L^{1}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau\lesssim\Vert f\Vert_{\dot {BV}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}.   \label{PJ6.13}
\end{equation}

Next we focus on the lower estimate of \eqref{PJ6.6}. To achieve this, we
will apply the following converse estimate to \eqref{PJ.14} that may be
found in \cite[Theorem 7, (4.3)]{W} 
\begin{equation}
\omega_{4}(f,\sqrt{t})_{1}\lesssim K(t,f;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot {H}^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})).   \label{PJ6.15}
\end{equation}
Moreover, in the special case that $f=\mathbf{1}_{E}$ for a set $E\subset 
\mathbb{R}^{N}$ with $|E| < \infty$, it is routine to check that 
\begin{equation*}
|\Delta_{h}\mathbf{1}_{E}(x)|\leq|\Delta_{h}^{4}\mathbf{1}_{E}(x)| 
\end{equation*}
for every $x,h\in\mathbb{R}^{N}$ and then 
\begin{equation*}
\omega_{1}(\mathbf{1}_{E},t)_{1}\leq\omega_{4}(\mathbf{1}_{E},t)_{1},\qquad 
\forall t>0. 
\end{equation*}
Thanks to \eqref{PD28.1}, we obtain 
\begin{equation}
K(t,\mathbf{1}_{E};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}))\lesssim\omega_{4}(\mathbf{1}_{E},t)_{1},\qquad\forall t>0. 
\label{PJ6.16}
\end{equation}
Putting together \eqref{PJ6.15} and \eqref{PJ6.16}, we have 
\begin{equation*}
K(\sqrt{t},\mathbf{1}_{E};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}))\lesssim K(t,\mathbf{1}_{E};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{H} 
^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})), 
\end{equation*}
which shows that the converse estimate of \eqref{PJ6.17} also holds for
characteristic functions associated to  sets of finite measure (but not for general functions in $L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$). Consequently, from 
\eqref{PJ6.9}, we get 
\begin{equation*}
\int_0^\infty \frac{\Vert H_{t}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}-\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau\gtrsim  \int_0^\infty \frac{K(\sqrt{t},\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}), 
\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau. 
\end{equation*}
Then a simple application of the Fatou's lemma in the setting of $\Gamma $ 
-convergence leads to 
\begin{align}
\Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow0}\int_0^\infty \frac{ 
\Vert H_{t}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}-\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{L^{1}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau & \nonumber \\
& \hspace{-6cm} \gtrsim\Gamma _{L^{1}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow0}\int_0^\infty \frac {K(\sqrt{t},\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau}^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{\sqrt{t 
}}\,d\tau  \notag \\
&\hspace{-6cm}  \geq\int_0^\infty \Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow
0}\frac{K(t,\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t}\,d\tau,   \label{PJ6.19}
\end{align}
where we have also applied a change of variables ($\sqrt{t}\leftrightarrow t$ 
) in the last step. Invoke now Theorem \ref{ThmGKFunct} with $ 
(A_{0},A_{1})=(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))$ and  
\eqref{PJ1.1} to find that 
\begin{equation*}
\Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\lim_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{K(t,\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau}^{f}};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t} 
=\Vert\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{G_{1}(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot {W} 
^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}\approx\Vert\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert _{\dot{BV} 
(\mathbb{R}^{N})}
\end{equation*}
with equivalence constants that are independent of $f$ and $\tau$. Using
this estimate into \eqref{PJ6.19} and applying \eqref{PJ6.12}, we get 
\begin{equation}
\Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow0}\int_0^\infty \frac{ 
\Vert H_{t}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}-\mathbf{1}_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{L^{1}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}}{\sqrt{t}}\,d\tau\gtrsim\int_0^\infty \Vert\mathbf{1} 
_{E_{\tau}^{f}}\Vert_{\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\,d\tau=\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{BV} 
(\mathbb{R}^{N})}.   \label{PJ6.20}
\end{equation}

Finally, by \eqref{PJ6.6}, \eqref{PJ6.13}, and \eqref{PJ6.20}, we  
conclude that 
\begin{align*}
\Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}} 
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|)(x)\,dx & \\
& \hspace{-5cm}\approx\Gamma_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\limsup_{t\rightarrow 0} 
\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|)(x)\,dx\approx\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}
\end{align*}
for $0 \leq f \in BV(\mathbb{R}^N)$. 

The general case $f \in BV(\mathbb{R}^N)$ follows from the previous case via standard arguments based on the  decomposition $f = f^{+}-f^{-}$ with $f^{+}, f^{-} \geq 0$. Note that $$\|f\|_{\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^N)} = \|f^{+}\|_{\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^N)}+ \|f^{-}\|_{\dot{BV}(\mathbb{R}^N)}$$ and (cf. \eqref{uba}) 
$$
	\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|)(x)\,dx= \int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f^{+}-f^{+}(x)|)(x)\,dx  +  \int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f^{-}-f^{-}(x)|)(x)\,dx.  
$$
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- theorem-18: body_md -->
\label{TheoremJ22.5} Let $p\in\lbrack1,\infty)$. If $f\in W^{1,p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})$ then 
\begin{align}
 \Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p} &\lesssim \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{t^{p/2}}\int_{ 
\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx   \notag \\
& \leq \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\limsup_{t\rightarrow 0} 
\frac{1}{t^{p/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx\lesssim \Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}   \label{PJ21.2}
\end{align}
and 
\begin{align}
\Vert f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p} &\lesssim \Gamma_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow\infty}\int_{ 
\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx   \notag \\
& \leq \Gamma_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\limsup
_{t\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx\lesssim 
\Vert f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}.   \label{PJ22.4}
\end{align}
<!-- end theorem-18 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
The $\Gamma$-limit in \eqref{PJ21.2} was already obtained in \cite[Theorem
1.6]{GS} by a different approach. On the other hand, \eqref{PJ22.4} is apparently new (in particular, in view of the method presented in Section \ref{SectionExact} below,  it seems plausible  to update its mode of $\Gamma$-convergence to $L^p$ and compute exact formulae).
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Given $t>0$, we can apply simple changes of variables and Fubini's theorem
to get 
\begin{align*}
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx & =\int_{\mathbb{R} 
^{N}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{t}(\xi)|f(x+\xi)-f(x)|^{p}\,d\xi\,dx \\
& =\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{t}(\xi)\Vert\Delta_{\xi}f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}^{p}\,d\xi \\
& =\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{1}(\xi)\Vert\Delta_{\sqrt{t}\xi}f\Vert _{L^{p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}\,d\xi.
\end{align*}
As a consequence, we derive 
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{t^{p/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx=\int_{ 
\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{1}(\xi)\bigg(\frac{\Vert\Delta_{\sqrt{t}\xi}f\Vert _{L^{p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}}{\sqrt{t}}\bigg)^{p}\,d\xi.   \label{PJ11.3}
\end{equation}

For each fixed $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\backslash\{0\}$, let us denote by $\dot{ 
W}_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$ the directional Sobolev space equipped with 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}:=\Vert D_{\xi}f\Vert
_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}, 
\end{equation*}
where $D_{\xi}f(x) := \langle \nabla f(x), \xi \rangle$. We also let 
\begin{equation*}
T_{t,\xi}f(x):=f(x+t\xi) 
\end{equation*}
for $t>0$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}$. It is plain to see that $\{T_{t,\xi
}\}_{t>0}$ defines a semigroup of linear operators acting on $L^{p}(\mathbb{R 
}^{N})$ with corresponding generator $\Lambda_{\xi}f=D_{\xi}f.$ In
particular, the domain of $\Lambda_{\xi}$ coincides with $\dot{W} 
_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$, more precisely, 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert f\Vert_{D(\Lambda_{\xi})}=\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}. 
\end{equation*}
By virtue of \cite[Theorem 6.7.3]{BL}, we can characterize the $K$ 
-functional relative to $(L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),D(\Lambda_{\xi}))=(L^{p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))$ as 
\begin{equation}
K(t,f;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))\approx
\sup_{0<s\leq t}\Vert T_{s,\xi}f-f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}=\sup_{0<s\leq t} 
\Vert\Delta_{s\xi}f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})},   \label{PJ11.1}
\end{equation}
where the hidden equivalence constant may be taken to be independent of $\xi$.

                                                                                                        
                                  
                                                                                                                                          
                

Using \eqref{PJ11.1} in the formula \eqref{PJ11.3}, we obtain the following
estimate 
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{t^{p/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx\lesssim
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{1}(\xi)\bigg(\frac{K(\sqrt{t},f;L^{p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}),\dot{W}_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{\sqrt{t}}\bigg)^{p}\,d\xi. 
\label{PJ11.4}
\end{equation}
Moreover, by \eqref{sec:sera} and Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, 
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{t^{p/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx &  \lesssim \int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{1}(\xi)\sup_{t > 0}\bigg(\frac{K(\sqrt{t},f;L^{p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N}),\dot{W}_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{\sqrt{t}}\bigg)^{p}\,d\xi \nonumber \\
& = \int_{\mathbb{R}^N} h_1(\xi) \|f\|_{G_1(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{W}^{1, p}_\xi(\mathbb{R}^N))}^p  \, d \xi\ \nonumber\\
&\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{1}(\xi)\Vert f\Vert_{\dot {W} 
_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}\,d\xi \nonumber \\ 
&\leq\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{1}(\xi)|\xi|^{p}\,d\xi 
\bigg)\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}.   \label{PJ11.4a}
\end{align}
Since $h_{1}(\xi)$ is a radial function (recall \eqref{DefHeatKernel}), we
can apply polar coordinates together with a simple change of variables to show the finiteness of the last integral,
more precisely, 
\begin{equation}
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}h_{1}(\xi)|\xi|^{p}\,d\xi=c_{N}\int_{0}^{ 
\infty}h_{1}(u)u^{p+N-1}\,du=c_{N}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{ 
\infty}e^{-u^{2}}u^{p+N-1}\,du<\infty.   \label{PJ11.5}
\end{equation}
In particular, by \eqref{PJ11.4a} and \eqref{PJ11.5}, 
\begin{align}
\Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\limsup_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{t^{p/2}}\int_{ 
\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx &  \notag \\
& \hspace{-6cm}\lesssim\limsup_{t\rightarrow0} \frac{1}{t^{p/2}}\int_{ 
\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx  \lesssim \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}.
\label{PJ11.6}
\end{align}

On the other hand, by \eqref{PJ11.3}, the monotonicity of heat kernels (cf.  
\eqref{DefHeatKernel}), and a simple change of variables, we have 
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{t^{p/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx & \geq
\int_{|\xi|<1}h_{1}(\xi)\bigg(\frac{\Vert\Delta_{\sqrt{t}\xi}f\Vert _{L^{p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})}}{\sqrt{t}}\bigg)^{p}\,d\xi \nonumber \\
& \gtrsim\frac{1}{t^{\frac{p+N}{2}}}\int_{|\xi|<\sqrt{t}}\Vert\Delta_{\xi
}f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}\,d\xi. \label{20.9.3}
\end{align}
Then, by virtue of \eqref{PD28.1} (with $k=1,q=p$ and $X=L^{p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})$), the previous estimate reads as 
\begin{equation*}
\frac{1}{t^{p/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx\gtrsim  
\bigg(\frac{K(\sqrt{t},f;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})) 
}{\sqrt{t}}\bigg)^{p}.  
\end{equation*}
We are now in a position to invoke \eqref{PJ22.2} with $(A_{0},A_{1})=(L^{p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))$ so that 
\begin{align}
\Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{t^{p/2}}\int_{ 
\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx &  \notag \\
& \hspace{-5.5cm}\gtrsim\Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow
0}\bigg(\frac{K(t,f;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t} 
\bigg)^{p}  \notag \\
& \hspace{-5.5cm}=\Vert f\Vert_{G_{1}(L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}^{1,p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N}))}^{p}\approx\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}^{p},   \label{PJ21.1}
\end{align}
where the last step follows from \eqref{PD28.5i}.

Putting together \eqref{PJ11.6} and \eqref{PJ21.1}, we arrive at the desired
estimates  \eqref{PJ21.2}. On the other hand, the proof of  
\eqref{PJ22.4} can be obtained in a similar fashion but now relying instead
on \eqref{PJ22.3}.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- theorem-19: body_md -->
Let $s\in(0,1)$ and $p\in[1,\infty)$ with\footnote{We restrict our attention to the most interesting case $2 s \geq p$. We mention that, for the remaining range $2 s < p$, somewhat expected limit formulae hold, cf. \cite[Theorem 1.7]{GS}.} $2s\geq p$. If $f\in W^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$ then 
\begin{align*}
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p} & \lesssim \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{\psi_{s,p}(t)} 
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}^{s}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx   \\
& \leq  \Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\limsup_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{\psi
_{s,p}(t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}^{s}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx \lesssim \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p},
\end{align*}
where 
\begin{equation*}
\psi_{s,p}(t)=\left\{ 
\begin{array}{cl}
t^{\frac{p}{2s}} &\qquad  \text{if}\qquad2s>p, \\ 
t|\log t| &\qquad  \text{if}\qquad2s=p. 
\end{array}
\right. 
\end{equation*}
<!-- end theorem-19 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
The proof in the case $2s>p$ follows line by line the argument given in the
proof of Theorem \ref{TheoremJ22.5} and then it is omitted. Therefore, we
now focus on the limiting case $2s=p$, where some changes, albeit still
minor, are needed.

Assume that $2s=p$. Applying a simple change of variables, we can write 
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{\psi_{s,p}(t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}^{s}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x) 
\,dx=t^{\frac{N}{2s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{h_{t}^{s}(t^{\frac{1}{2s} 
}\xi)}{\psi_{s,p}(t)}\Vert\Delta_{t^{\frac{1}{2s}}\xi}f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R 
}^{N})}^{p}\,d\xi.   \label{PJ25.2}
\end{equation}
Accordingly, by \eqref{PJ11.1}, we have 
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{\psi_{s,p}(t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}^{s}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx 
\lesssim I+II+III,   \label{PJ25.6}
\end{equation}
where 
\begin{equation*}
I:=\frac{1}{|\log t|}\int_{|\xi|<1}\frac{1}{(1+|\xi|^{2})^{\frac{N+2s}{2}}} 
\bigg(\frac{K(t^{\frac{1}{p}},f;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W}_{\xi}^{1,p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t^{\frac{1}{p}}}\bigg)^{p}\,d\xi, 
\end{equation*} 
\begin{equation*}
II:=\frac{1}{|\log t|}\int_{1<|\xi|<\frac{1}{t}}\frac{1}{(1+|\xi|^{2})^{ 
\frac{N+2s}{2}}}\bigg(\frac{K(t^{\frac{1}{p}},f;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W} 
_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t^{\frac{1}{p}}}\bigg)^{p}\,d\xi, 
\end{equation*}
and 
\begin{equation*}
III:=\frac{1}{|\log t|}\int_{|\xi|>\frac{1}{t}}\frac{1}{(1+|\xi|^{2})^{\frac{ 
N+2s}{2}}}\bigg(\frac{K(t^{\frac{1}{p}},f;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\dot{W} 
_{\xi}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N}))}{t^{\frac{1}{p}}}\bigg)^{p}\,d\xi. 
\end{equation*}
Here, without loss of generality, we may assume that $t<1$. We now
estimate each of these three terms. Firstly,  $I$ can be controlled using ideas
similar to those that led to \eqref{PJ11.4}: 
\begin{equation}
I\leq\frac{1}{|\log t|}\bigg(\int_{|\xi|<1}\frac{|\xi|^{p}}{(1+|\xi |^{2})^{ 
\frac{N+2s}{2}}}\,d\xi\bigg)\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}^{p}\approx\frac{1}{|\log t|}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}^{p}.   \label{PJ25.7}
\end{equation}
To estimate $III$, we use \eqref{sec:sera} to get 
\begin{align}
	III &\leq \frac{1}{t |\log t|} \int_{|\xi| > \frac{1}{t}} \frac{1}{(1+|\xi|^2)^{\frac{N+2 s}{2}}} \sup_{t > 0} K(t^{\frac{1}{p}}, f; L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{W}^{1, p}_\xi(\mathbb{R}^N))^p \, d \xi \nonumber \\
	& =   \frac{1}{t |\log t|} \int_{|\xi| > \frac{1}{t}} \frac{1}{(1+|\xi|^2)^{\frac{N+2 s}{2}}} \|f\|_{G_0(L^p(\mathbb{R}^N), \dot{W}^{1, p}_\xi(\mathbb{R}^N))}^p \, d \xi \nonumber \\
	& \leq \frac{1}{t |\log t|}  \bigg( \int_{|\xi| > \frac{1}{t}} \frac{1}{(1+|\xi|^2)^{\frac{N+2 s}{2}}}  \, d \xi  \bigg) \|f\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)}^p \nonumber \\
	& \approx  \frac{1}{t |\log t|}  \bigg( \int_{|\xi| > \frac{1}{t}} \frac{1}{|\xi|^{N+ 2 s}}  \, d \xi  \bigg) \|f\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)}^p  \approx 
\frac{t^{p-1}}{|\log t|}\Vert f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}. \label{PJ25.8}
\end{align}
Finally, we can estimate $II$ as follows: 
\begin{equation}
II\lesssim\frac{1}{|\log t|}\bigg(\int_{1<|\xi|<\frac{1}{t}}\frac{1}{|\xi
|^{N}}\,d\xi\bigg)\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}\approx 
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}.   \label{PJ25.9}
\end{equation}
Inserting \eqref{PJ25.7}--\eqref{PJ25.9} into \eqref{PJ25.6}, we arrive at 
\begin{equation*}
\frac{1}{\psi_{s,p}(t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}^{s}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx 
\lesssim\bigg(1+\frac{1}{|\log t|}\bigg)\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R 
}^{N})}^{p}+\frac{t^{p-1}}{|\log t|}\Vert f\Vert _{L^{p}(\mathbb{R} 
^{N})}^{p}. 
\end{equation*}
Consequently, 
\begin{align}
\Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\limsup_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{\psi_{s,p}(t)} 
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}^{s}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx &  \notag \\
& \hspace{-6cm}\lesssim\limsup_{t\rightarrow0}\bigg[\bigg(1+\frac{1}{|\log t| 
}\bigg)\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}+\frac{t^{p-1}}{|\log
t|}\Vert f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}\bigg]=\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W} 
^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}.   \label{PJ26.3}
\end{align}

Conversely, by \eqref{PJ25.2} and a change of variables we see that 
\begin{equation}\label{PJ26.1}
\frac{1}{\psi_{s,p}(t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}^{s}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx 
\gtrsim\frac{1}{|\log t|}\int_{t^{\frac{1}{p}}<|\xi|<1}\frac { 
\Vert\Delta_{\xi}f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}}{|\xi|^{N+p}}\,d\xi. 
\end{equation}
Moreover, the $\Gamma$-limit of the right-hand side of \eqref{PJ26.1} is a
prototypical example of the classical BBM formula, namely, 
\begin{equation}
\Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\lim_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{|\log t|} 
\int_{t<|\xi|<1}\frac{\Vert\Delta_{\xi}f\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}}{ 
|\xi|^{N+p}}\,d\xi=c\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}; 
\label{PJ26.2}
\end{equation}
cf. \cite[Section 6.7.5]{BM}. Then it follows from \eqref{PJ26.1} and  
\eqref{PJ26.2} that 
\begin{equation*}
\Gamma_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}-\liminf_{t\rightarrow0}\frac{1}{\psi_{s,p}(t)} 
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}H_{t}^{s}(|f-f(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx\gtrsim\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{ 
W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}, 
\end{equation*}
which combined with \eqref{PJ26.3} gives the desired result in the limiting
case $2s=p$.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
The formula  \eqref{PJ26.2} is a special case of the general BBM formulation: 
	\begin{equation}\label{16.9.1}
		\Gamma_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)} - \lim_{t \to 0} \int_{\mathbb{R}^N} \|\Delta_\xi f\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)}^p \phi_t(|\xi|) \, d \xi = c \Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{p}
	\end{equation}
	for a suitable family $\{\phi_t\}$ of nonnegative kernels. One can show that  \eqref{16.9.1} is also part of a general extrapolation phenomenon in the spirit of Theorem \ref{Thm1}, but now formulated in terms of  non-standard interpolation weights, cf. \cite{DLTYY}. However, this extension requires some further technicalities and we believe it deserves a separate study.
<!-- end remark-13 -->
<!-- theorem-20: body_md -->
\label{Cwikelsthm} Let $(A_{0},A_{1})$ and $(B_{0},B_{1})$ be interpolation
pairs of Banach spaces and let $T:A_{0}+A_{1}\longrightarrow B_{0}+B_{1}$ be
a linear operator such that $T:A_{0}\longrightarrow B_{0}$ is bounded and $ 
T:A_{1}\longrightarrow B_{1}$ is compact. Then for any $\theta \in (0, 1)$ and $p \in [1, \infty]$,
\begin{equation*}
T:(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}\longrightarrow(B_{0},B_{1})_{\theta,p}  
\end{equation*}
is compact.
<!-- end theorem-20 -->
<!-- theorem-21: body_md -->
\label{Thm5 copy(1)} Let $(A_{0},A_{1})$ and $(B_{0},B_{1})$ be
interpolation pairs of Banach spaces. Let $T:A_{0}+A_{1}\longrightarrow
B_{0}+B_{1}$ be a linear operator such that $T:A_{0}\longrightarrow B_{0}$
is bounded and $T:A_{1}\longrightarrow B_{1}$ is compact. Let $ 
p\in\lbrack1,\infty ],\{\theta_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}\subset(0,1)$ with $\lim_{n\rightarrow 
\infty}\theta_{n}=1$, and let $\{u_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ be a sequence such that 
\begin{equation}
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Vert
u_{n}\Vert_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta_{n},p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}<\infty. 
\label{vuelta1}
\end{equation}
Then given any $\eta\in(0,1)$ there exists $u\in B_{1}+(B_{0},B_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}
$ such that $Tu_{n}\rightarrow u$ in $B_{1}+(B_{0},B_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}$ as $ 
n\rightarrow\infty$ (up to a subsequence).
<!-- end theorem-21 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
We will make use of the following extrapolation estimate (cf. \cite[ 
Corollary 2.1]{KM}): Let $0<\eta<\theta<1$ then for all $f\in(A_{0},A_{1})_{ 
\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}$ we have 
\begin{equation}
\Vert f\Vert_{A_{1}+(A_{0},A_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}\leq M\Vert
f\Vert_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}},   \label{PF3.1}
\end{equation}
where $M$ is a positive constant independent of $\theta$ and $\eta$. Now, on
account that $\theta_{n}\rightarrow1$ as $n\rightarrow\infty$, it is
possible to choose $\eta\in(0,1)$ such that $\eta<\theta_{n}$ for every $ 
n\in {\mathbb{N}}$. Consequently,  by \eqref{PF3.1}, 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert f\Vert_{A_{1}+(A_{0},A_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}\leq M\Vert
f\Vert_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta_{n},p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}
\end{equation*}
for all $f\in(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta_{n},p}^{\blacktriangleleft}$. Note that
