since the constant $M$ is independent of $n$, we can apply the previous
inequality to the $u_{n}$, 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert u_{n}\Vert_{A_{1}+(A_{0},A_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}\leq
M\Vert
u_{n}\Vert_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta_{n},p}^{\blacktriangleleft}},\qquad\forall
n\in{\mathbb{N}},
\end{equation*}
which combined with \eqref{vuelta1} yields 
\begin{equation}
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Vert
u_{n}\Vert_{A_{1}+(A_{0},A_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}}<\infty.   \label{P5.1}
\end{equation}

Since $T$ satisfies
the assumptions of Cwikel's theorem (cf. Theorem \ref{Cwikelsthm}), 
\begin{equation}\label{16.9.2}
T:(A_{0},A_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft} \longrightarrow(B_{0},B_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}
\end{equation}
is a compact operator. It follows from \eqref{16.9.2} and the assumption $T: A_1 \longrightarrow B_1$ is compact  that
\begin{equation}\label{16.9.3}
T:A_{1}+(A_{0},A_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}\longrightarrow B_{1}+(B_{0},B_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft} 
\end{equation}
is also compact. Using (\ref{P5.1}), we may select a bounded subsequence (still named $ 
\{u_{n}\}_{n\in \mathbb{N}})$ in $A_{1}+(A_{0},A_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}$.  In view of \eqref{16.9.3} we derive that $\{Tu_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ is precompact in $B_{1}+(B_{0},B_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}$ and
consequently there exists a subsequence $\{u_{n_{k}}\}_{k \in \mathbb{N}}$ such that $ 
\{Tu_{n_{k}}\}_{k \in \mathbb{N}}$ converges in $B_{1}+(B_{0},B_{1})_{\eta,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}$.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- theorem-22: body_md -->
\label{Thm5**} Let $(A_{0},A_{1})$ and $(B_{0},B_{1})$ be
interpolation pairs of normed spaces.  Suppose that $T: A_0 + A_1 \longrightarrow B_0 + B_1$ is a linear operator such that $ 
T:A_{0}\longrightarrow B_0$ is bounded and $T:A_{1}\longrightarrow B_1$ is
compact. Let $\{t_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ be an infinitesimal and let $\{u_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ be a
sequence such that 
\begin{equation}
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(t_{n},u_{n};A_{0},A_{1})}{t_{n}} 
<\infty.   \label{PF5.1}
\end{equation}
Then there exists $v\in B_1$ such that $Tu_{n}\rightarrow v$
in $B_0 + B_1$ as $n\rightarrow\infty$ (up to a subsequence).
<!-- end theorem-22 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Let 
$$
	M:= \liminf_{n \to \infty} \frac{K(t_n, u_n; A_0, A_1)}{t_n}.
$$
According to \eqref{PF5.1}, there exist subsequences of $\{t_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ and $\{u_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ such that 
\begin{equation}\label{PF5.1*}
	\lim_{n \to \infty}  \frac{K(t_n, u_n; A_0, A_1)}{t_n} = M. 
\end{equation}
	For every $n \in \mathbb{N}$, there exists a decomposition $u_n = w_n + v_n \in A_0 + A_1$ such that
	\begin{equation}\label{PF5.2}
		\|w_n\|_{A_0} + t_n \|v_n\|_{A_1} \leq K(t_n, u_n; A_0, A_1) + t_n.
	\end{equation}
	In particular, 
	\begin{equation}\label{PF5.2*}
		\|v_n\|_{A_1} \leq  \frac{K(t_n, u_n; A_0, A_1)}{t_n} + 1
	\end{equation}
	and then, by virtue of \eqref{PF5.1*}, there exists a subsequence of $\{v_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ (which is relabelled to simplify the notation) uniformly bounded in $A_1$.  By the compactness of $T: A_1 \longrightarrow B_1$, there exists $v \in B_1$ and a subsequence of $\{v_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$  such that 
	\begin{equation}\label{PF13.6}
		\lim_{n \to \infty} T v_n = v \quad \text{in} \quad B_1.
		\end{equation}
		 On the other hand, from the assumption that $T: A_0 \longrightarrow B_0$,  \eqref{PF5.2} and \eqref{PF5.1*}, we also derive  
	\begin{equation}\label{20.9.1}
	\|T w_n\|_{B_0}	\lesssim \|w_n\|_{A_0}  \leq K(t_n, u_n; A_0, A_1) + t_n \lesssim t_n 
	\end{equation}
	and hence $T w_n \to 0$ in $B_0$ as $n \to \infty$. Therefore, from the estimate 
	$$
		\|T u_n - v\|_{B_0 + B_1} \leq \|T w_n \|_{B_0} + \|T v_n -v\|_{B_1}
	$$
	and \eqref{PF13.6}, \eqref{20.9.1}, we derive that   $T u_n \to v$ in $B_0+B_1$ as $n \to \infty$.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- remark-14: body_md -->
\label{RemarkThm5**}
	Let us focus on    Theorem \ref{Thm5**} with $B_0 = B_1 = A_0$, i.e., $T: A_0 \longrightarrow A_0$ and $T: A_1 \longrightarrow A_0$.  Assume further that $T: A_1 \longrightarrow A_1$ boundedly and the norm on $A_1$ is lower semicontinuous with respect to the convergence in $A_0$. Then Theorem \ref{Thm5**} can be reinforced to ensure that the limit $v \in A_0 \cap A_1$. Indeed, by taking limits in \eqref{PF5.2*}, we know that $\liminf_{n \to \infty} \|v_n\|_{A_1} < \infty$ and hence $\liminf_{n \to \infty} \|T v_n\|_{A_1} < \infty$. Since $T v_n \to v$ in $A_0$ (cf. \eqref{PF13.6}), we get $\|v\|_{A_1} \leq \liminf_{n \to \infty} \|T v_n\|_{A_1} < \infty$.
<!-- end remark-14 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{CorCmp1} Let $X$ be a r.i. space with absolutely continuous norm,
defined on a cube $Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{N}$. Let $q\in\lbrack1,\infty]$ and $ 
k\in{\mathbb{N}}$. Suppose that $\{f_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ is a bounded sequence in $X$
such that\footnote{ 
The Besov spaces $\dot{B}_{q}^{s k}X$ on $Q$ are defined in a similar fashion
as their counterparts on $\mathbb{R}^{N}$ (cf. \eqref{DefBesovD}): $\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{B}_{q}^{sk}X}=(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}|h|^{-skq-N}\Vert 
\Delta_{h}^{k}f\Vert_{X(Q_{kh})}^{q}\,dh)^{1/q}$, where $Q_{kh}=\{x\in
Q:x+kh\in Q\}$.} 
\begin{equation}
\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}(1-s_{n})^{1/q}\Vert f_{n}\Vert_{\dot{B} 
_{q}^{s_{n}k}X}<\infty   \label{PF13.3}
\end{equation}
for some sequence $s_{n}\rightarrow1^{-}$. Then there exists $f\in X$ such
that $f_{n}\rightarrow f$ in $X$ as $n\rightarrow\infty$ (up to a
subsequence).
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- remark-15: body_md -->
This result extends the classical BBM compactness criteria for the fractional
Sobolev spaces $\dot{W}^{s,p}$ (cf. \cite[Theorem 4]{BBM2})$,$ that
corresponds to the choice $X=L^{p},p\in\lbrack1,\infty),q=p$ and $k=1$ (cf.  
\eqref{CoincGFS}).
<!-- end remark-15 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
We apply Theorem  \ref{Thm5 copy(1)}  to the pairs $(A_{0},A_{1})=(X,W^{k}X), (B_0, B_1) = (X, X)$ and $T =\text{id}$. Since $X
$ has absolutely continuous norm, the embedding $W^{k}X\hookrightarrow X$ is
compact (cf. \cite{Pu}). It is easy to see that, for any interpolation pair $ 
(A_{0},A_{1})$, $t\in(0,1)$ and $f\in A_{0}$, we have 
\begin{equation}
K(t,f;A_{0},A_{0}\cap A_{1})\approx t\Vert
f\Vert_{A_{0}}+K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1}).   \label{PF13.1}
\end{equation}
Under the assumption $A_{1}\hookrightarrow A_{0}$, we have 
\begin{equation}
\Vert f\Vert_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{\theta,q}}\approx\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}[t^{-\theta
}K(t,f;A_{0},A_{1})]^{q}\,\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}   \label{PF13.2}
\end{equation}
with equivalence constants that are independent of $\theta\rightarrow1^{-}$,
cf. \cite[Lemma 3]{M-Lim}. Using \eqref{PF13.1} and \eqref{PF13.2}, we
compute 
\begin{align*}
\Vert f\Vert_{(X,W^{k}X)_{\theta,q}} & \approx\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}[t^{-\theta
}K(t,f;X,W^{k}X)]^{q}\,\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q} \\
& \approx\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}[t^{-\theta}K(t,f;X,\dot{W}^{k}X)]^{q}\,\frac {dt 
}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}t^{(1-\theta)q}\,\frac{dt}{t}\bigg) 
^{1/q}\Vert f\Vert_{X} \\
& \lesssim\Vert f\Vert_{(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)_{\theta,q}}+(1-\theta)^{-1/q}\Vert
f\Vert_{X},
\end{align*}
where the constants on each step are independent of $\theta\rightarrow1^{-}$ 
. In particular, with constants independent of $n\in \mathbb{N},$ 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert f_{n}\Vert_{(X,W^{k}X)_{s_{n},q}}\lesssim\Vert f_{n}\Vert_{(X,\dot {W} 
^{k}X)_{s_{n},q}}+(1-s_{n})^{-1/q}\Vert f_{n}\Vert_{X}. 
\end{equation*}
Using \eqref{PD28.3} adapted to $Q$, the previous estimate can be expressed
as 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert f_{n}\Vert_{(X,W^{k}X)_{s_{n},q}}\lesssim\Vert f_{n}\Vert_{\dot{B} 
_{q}^{s_{n}k}X}+(1-s_{n})^{-1/q}\Vert f_{n}\Vert_{X}
\end{equation*}
and recalling the normalization constants $ 
c_{s_{n},q}=(s_{n}(1-s_{n})q)^{1/q}$:  
\begin{equation*}
\Vert
f_{n}\Vert_{(X,W^{k}X)_{s_{n},q}^{\blacktriangleleft}}=c_{s_{n},q}\Vert
f_{n}\Vert_{(X,W^{k}X)_{s_{n},q}}\lesssim(1-s_{n})^{1/q}\Vert f_{n}\Vert _{ 
\dot{B}_{q}^{s_{n}k}X}+\Vert f_{n}\Vert_{X}. 
\end{equation*}
In view of \eqref{PF13.3} and the boundedness of $\{f_{n}\}_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ in $X$ 
, condition \eqref{vuelta1} is satisfied and we can apply Theorem \ref{Thm5 copy(1)} 
to conclude.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{CorCompactAR} Assume that $\{\varepsilon_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ is infinitesimal and
let $\{f_{n}\}_{n\in \mathbb{N}}\subset L_{\emph{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$ be such
that 
\begin{equation}
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\kappa_{\varepsilon_{n}}(f_{n})<\infty. 
\label{PF13.5}
\end{equation}
Then there exist a sequence $\{c_{n}\}_{n\in \mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}$ and $f\in
L_{\emph{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})\cap\dot{\emph{BV}}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$ such
that $(f_{n}-c_{n})\rightarrow f$ in $L_{\emph{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$
(up to a subsequence).
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
For simplicity, we shall restrict our attention to function spaces defined
over a cube $Q$. Consider the pairs, $(A_{0},A_{1})=(L^{1}(Q),\dot{BV} 
(Q)), (B_0, B_1) = (L^1(Q), L^1(Q))$ and the linear operator $Tf=f-f_{Q}$. Clearly, $T$ maps $ 
L^{1}(Q)$ into itself. On the other hand, by the classical
Rellich--Kondrachov theorem, $T:\dot{BV}(Q)\longrightarrow L^{1}(Q)$ is
compact. To be able to apply Theorem \ref{Thm5**} \ we need to verify that  
\eqref{PF5.1} holds: 
\begin{equation}
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(\varepsilon_{n},f_{n};L^{1}(Q),\dot {BV} 
(Q))}{\varepsilon_{n}}<\infty.   \label{PF13.4}
\end{equation}
To see this, we can make use of the trivial embedding $\dot{W} 
^{1,1}(Q)\hookrightarrow\dot{BV}(Q)$ and \eqref{K-Funct-DS}
(more precisely, its local counterpart) to get 
\begin{equation*}
\frac{K(\varepsilon_{n},f_{n};L^{1}(Q),\dot{BV}(Q))}{ 
\varepsilon_{n}}\lesssim\frac{K(\varepsilon_{n},f_{n};L^{1}(Q),\dot{W} 
^{1,1}(Q))}{\varepsilon_{n}}\approx\kappa_{\varepsilon_{n}}(f_{n}) 
\end{equation*}
and hence \eqref{PF13.4} follows from \eqref{PF13.5}. By virtue of Theorem  
\ref{Thm5**}, there exists $f\in L^{1}(Q)$ such that $ 
Tf_{n}=f_{n}-(f_{n})_{Q}\rightarrow f$ in $L^{1}(Q)$ as $n\rightarrow\infty$ 
. In addition, since $\Vert Tf\Vert_{\dot{BV}\text{(}Q)}=\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{ 
BV}(Q)}$ and the $\dot{BV}(Q)$ norm is lower semicontinuous with respect to
the $L^{1}(Q)$-convergence, it turns out that $f\in\dot{BV}(Q)$, cf. Remark \ref{RemarkThm5**}. This proves the desired result
with scalars $c_{n}=(f_{n})_{Q}$.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
Let $p\in[1,\infty)$. Assume that $\{t_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ is infinitesimal and let $ 
\{f_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ be a bounded sequence in $L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$ such that 
\begin{equation}
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{t_{n}^{p/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R} 
^{N}}H_{t_{n}}(|f_{n}-f_{n}(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx<\infty.   \label{PF6.2}
\end{equation}
Then (up to a subsequence) $f_{n}\rightarrow f$ in $L_{\emph{loc}}^{p}( 
\mathbb{R}^{N})$ for some $f\in L_{\emph{loc}}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$ (in addition, $f \in W^{1, p}_{\emph{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^N)$ if $p > 1$ and $f \in BV_{\emph{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^N)$ if $p=1$).
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
To avoid unnecessary technicalities, we shall restrict our attention to function spaces defined on a fixed cube $Q \subset \mathbb{R}^N$. It follows from \eqref{20.9.3} that 
$$
	\frac{1}{t_n^{\frac{p+N}{2}}}\int_{|\xi|<\sqrt{t_n}}\Vert\Delta_{\xi
}f_n\Vert_{L^{p}(Q_\xi)}^{p}\,d\xi \lesssim \frac{1}{t_{n}^{p/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R 
}^{N}}H_{t_{n}}(|f_{n}-f_{n}(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx, 
$$
which can be rewritten in terms of $K$-functionals (cf. \eqref{PD28.1}) as follows
\begin{equation*}
\bigg(\frac{K(\sqrt{t_{n}},f_{n};L^{p}(Q),\dot{W}^{1,p}(Q))}{\sqrt{t_{n}}}\bigg)^{p}\lesssim\frac{1}{t_{n}^{p/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R 
}^{N}}H_{t_{n}}(|f_{n}-f_{n}(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx 
\end{equation*}
for every $n\in{\mathbb{N}}$ and, in particular (cf. \eqref{PF13.1} and  
\eqref{PF6.2}), 
\begin{align*}
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(\sqrt{t_{n}},f_{n};L^{p}(Q),W^{1,p}(Q))}{\sqrt{t_{n}}} & \\
& \hspace{-4.5cm}\lesssim\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\Vert f_{n}\Vert _{L^{p}(Q)}+\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K(\sqrt{t_{n}} 
,f_{n};L^{p}(Q),\dot{W}^{1,p}(Q))}{\sqrt{t_{n}}} \\
& \hspace{-4.5cm} \lesssim \sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\Vert f_{n}\Vert _{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^N)} + \liminf_{n \to \infty}  \frac{1}{t_{n}^{1/2}} \bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R 
}^{N}}H_{t_{n}}(|f_{n}-f_{n}(x)|^{p})(x)\,dx \bigg)^{1/p} < \infty. 
\end{align*}
Then the desired convergence assertion is an immediate consequence of
Theorem \ref{Thm5**} with $(A_{0},A_{1})=(L^{p}(Q),W^{1,p}(Q)), (B_0, B_1) = (L^p(Q), L^p(Q))$ and $T= \text{id}$ on $Q$. Moreover, one can apply Remark \ref{RemarkThm5**} to conclude that $f \in W^{1, p}(Q)$ if $p > 1$. The case $p=1$ needs some further justifications: It is possible to replace the role of $W^{1, 1}(Q)$ by $BV(Q)$ in the above computations since $$G_0 (L^1(Q), W^{1, 1}(Q)) = L^1(Q) \qquad \text{and} \qquad  G_1(L^1(Q), W^{1, 1}(Q)) = BV(Q)$$ and then (cf. \eqref{sec2:5})
\begin{equation}\label{29.9.3}
K(t, f; L^1(Q), BV(Q)) \approx K(t, f; L^1(Q), W^{1, 1}(Q)).
\end{equation}
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
Let $p\in\lbrack1,\infty)$. Assume that $\{t_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ is infinitesimal and $ 
\{f_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}\subset L^{p}(\mathcal{X})$ such that 
\begin{equation}
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{E_{p,t_{n}}(f_{n})}{t_{n}} 
\bigg)^{p}+\Vert f_{n}\Vert_{L^{p}(\mathcal{X})}^{p}\bigg]<\infty. 
\label{PF8.1}
\end{equation}
Then $\{f_{n}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ contains a subsequence that converges strongly in $L^{p}( 
\mathcal{X})$.
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
We wish to apply Theorem \ref{Thm5**} for the choice $(A_{0}, A_{1}) =
(L^{p}(\mathcal{X}),L^{p}(\mathcal{X}) \cap KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{X})), (B_0, B_1) =(
L^{p}(\mathcal{X}), L^{p}(\mathcal{X}))$ and $T = \text{id}$ (the compactness of the embedding 
$L^{p}(\mathcal{X}) \cap KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{X}) \hookrightarrow L^{p}( 
\mathcal{X})$ is known, cf. \cite[(68)]{ARB}). From \eqref{PF13.1} and  
\eqref{PJ4.4}, 
\begin{equation*}
\frac{K(t, f; L^{p}(\mathcal{X}), L^{p}(\mathcal{X}) \cap KS^{1, p}(\mathcal{ 
X}))}{t} \approx\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathcal{X})} + \frac{E_{p, t}(f)}{t}
\end{equation*}
and hence the validity of \eqref{PF8.1} implies \eqref{PF5.1}.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- remark-16: body_md -->
\label{Remark15}
An alternative argument to Step 4 leading to convergence  in certain Banach function spaces that are
not uniformly convex including $L^{1}$, and even Orlicz spaces that satisfy
the $\Delta_{2}$ condition, is to dispense with weak convergence and replace
it by a.e. convergence through the use of the Brezis--Lieb lemma \cite 
{BrLi}. In fact,  the Brezis--Lieb lemma simply states that if $ 
f_{n}\rightarrow f$ a.e. and $\{f_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ is uniformly bounded in $L^p, p >0,$ then 
\begin{equation*}
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}(\left\Vert f_{n}\right\Vert _{L^{p}}^p-\left\Vert
f-f_{n}\right\Vert _{L^{p}}^p)=\left\Vert f\right\Vert _{L^{p}}^p. 
\end{equation*}
In particular, a.e. convergence and $\lim_{n \to \infty} \left\Vert f_{n}\right\Vert
_{L^{1}}=\left\Vert f\right\Vert _{L^{1}}$ yield norm convergence in $L^1$.

In the general setting that $B$ is a Banach function space, the above proof (cf. Step 3) reveals that (up to a subfamily) $\bar{u}_\theta \to \bar{u}$ as $\theta \to 1^{-}$ in $A_0$.
<!-- end remark-16 -->
<!-- theorem-23: body_md -->
\label{ThmGenMinimization} Let $B$ be a Banach function space that is uniformly convex and let $X$ be
a r.i. space with absolutely continuous norm such that $B \hookrightarrow X$. Let $q>0,p\in\lbrack1,\infty),k\in{\mathbb{N 
}}$ and $\lambda>0$. Assume that $f\in B$. For each $s\in(0,1)$, let $\bar{u} 
_{s}$ be a near-minimizer  for the functional 
\begin{equation*}
E_{s}(u):=\Vert f-u\Vert_{B}^{q}+\lambda(1-s)^{1/p}\Vert u\Vert_{\dot{B} 
_{p}^{sk}X}. 
\end{equation*}
Then (up to a subfamily) $\bar{u}_{s}\rightarrow\bar{u}$ in $B$ as $ 
s\rightarrow1^{-}$, where $\bar{u}$ is a minimizer of 
\begin{equation*}
E_1(u):=\Vert f-u\Vert_{B}^{q}+\lambda F(u) 
\end{equation*}
and\footnote{ 
Note that $F(u)\approx\Vert u\Vert_{\dot{W}^{k}X}$ if $u \in \dot{W}^k X$, cf. \eqref{PF12.1}.} 
\begin{equation*}
F(u):=\bigg(\frac{1}{kp}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{N-1}}\bigg\|\sum_{|\alpha|=k} \frac{k!}{\alpha!} \, 
\omega^{\alpha}D^{\alpha}u\bigg\|_{X}^{p}\,d\sigma^{N-1}(\omega)\bigg) 
^{1/p}. 
\end{equation*}
<!-- end theorem-23 -->
<!-- corollary-5: body_md -->
\label{CorMinSob} Let $B$ be a Banach function space that is uniformly convex  with $B \hookrightarrow L^p$ for some $p \in [1, \infty)$ (e.g., $B = L^r$ with\footnote{The case $B=L^1$ is also admissible, cf. Remark \ref{Remark15}.} $r \in [p, \infty)$). Let $q>0$ and $\lambda>0$. Assume that $f \in B$.  For
each $s\in(0,1)$, let $\bar{u}_{s}$ be a near-minimizer of 
\begin{equation*}
E_{s}(u):=\Vert f-u\Vert_{B}^{q}+\lambda(1-s)^{1/p}\Vert u\Vert_{\dot{W} 
^{s,p}}. 
\end{equation*}
Then (up to a subfamily) $\bar{u}_{s}\rightarrow\bar{u}$ in $B$ as $s\rightarrow1^{-}$, where $ 
\bar{u}$ is a minimizer of 
\begin{equation*}
E_1(u):=\Vert f-u\Vert_{B}^{q}+\lambda\bigg(\frac{C_{p,N}}{p}\bigg)^{1/p}F(u) 
\end{equation*}
with 
\begin{equation*}
F(u)=\left\{ 
\begin{array}{cl}
\Vert\nabla u\Vert_{L^{p}} & \qquad  \text{if}\qquad p\in(1,\infty), \\ 
\Vert u\Vert_{\dot{BV}} & \qquad  \text{if}\qquad p=1. 
\end{array}
\right. 
\end{equation*}
Here, the constant $C_{p,N}$ is given by \eqref{PF10.1}.
<!-- end corollary-5 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
One may be tempted to apply directly Theorem \ref{ThmVarIntro} (taking into account
the switch in the roles of the parameters $s$ and $\theta$) to the family of
functionals 
\begin{equation*}
\widetilde{E}_{s}(u):=\Vert f-u\Vert_{B}^{q}+\lambda \Vert u\Vert_{(X, 
\dot{W}^{k}X)_{s,p}^{\blacktriangleleft}},\qquad s\in(0,1), 
\end{equation*}
and 
\begin{equation*}
\widetilde{E}_1(u):=\Vert f-u\Vert_{B}^{q}+\lambda\Vert u\Vert_{G_{1}(X,\dot {W} 
^{k}X)}. 
\end{equation*}
In this regard, recall that $X \cap \dot{W}^k X = W^k X \hookrightarrow X$ compactly (cf. the proof of Corollary \ref{CorCmp1}). Unfortunately, we only have at our disposal the characterizations  
\eqref{PD28.3} and \eqref{PD28.5i}, namely, 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert u\Vert_{(X,\dot{W}^{k}X)_{s,p}}\approx\Vert u\Vert_{\dot{B} 
_{p}^{sk}X}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Vert u\Vert_{G_{1}(X,\dot{W} 
^{k}X)}\approx\Vert u\Vert_{\dot{W}^{k}X}
\end{equation*}
and hence 
\begin{equation*}
E_{s}(u)\approx\widetilde{E}_{s}(u)\qquad\text{and}\qquad E_1(u)\approx 
\widetilde{E}_1(u). 
\end{equation*}
In principle, the asymptotic behavior of near-minimizers of $\widetilde{E} 
_{s}$ may be quite different from the corresponding behavior of near-minimizers of $E_{s}$. But it is possible to circumvent the obstruction
following, line by line, the proof of Theorem \ref{ThmVarIntro} but now applying the
exact formulae derived in Section \ref{SectionExact} (more precisely,  
\eqref{Pdjjsjas3}) and Corollary \ref{CorCmp1}.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- theorem-24: body_md -->
\label{Thm7new} Suppose  that $\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}$ is a near-minimizer of $ 
\mathfrak{F}_{\varepsilon}$. Then (up to a subfamily) $\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\bar{u}$
in $B$ as $\varepsilon\rightarrow0^{+}$, where $\bar{u}$ is a minimizer of
the functional $E_1$ defined by \eqref{23.9.4} and $E_1(\bar{u} 
)=\lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow0^{+}}\mathfrak{F}_{\varepsilon}(\bar{u} 
_{\varepsilon}).$
<!-- end theorem-24 -->
<!-- corollary-6: body_md -->
\label{CoroIP1} Let $f\in L^{r},r\in\lbrack1,\infty),$ and $q, \lambda>0$. For each $ 
s\in(0,1)$, let $\bar{u}_{s}$ be a near-minimizer of 
\begin{equation*}
E_{s}(u)=\Vert f-u\Vert_{L^{r}}^{q}+\frac{\lambda}{4}(1-s)\Vert u\Vert _{ 
\dot{W}^{s,1}}.   
\end{equation*}
Then there exists $\bar{u}\in L^{r}$ such that $\bar{u}_{s}\rightarrow\bar{u}
$ (up to a subfamily) in $L^{r}$ as $s\rightarrow1^{-}$, where $\bar{u}$ is a
minimizer of 
\begin{equation}
E_1(u)=\Vert f-u\Vert_{L^{r}}^{q}+\lambda\Vert u\Vert_{\dot{BV}}. 
\label{PF15.1}
\end{equation}
In particular, \eqref{PF15.1} with $r=q=2$ coincides with \eqref{IP2} and  
\eqref{PF15.1} with $r=q=1$ coincides with \eqref{IP3}.
<!-- end corollary-6 -->
<!-- remark-17: body_md -->
Given $Q_{0}$ a cube in $\mathbb{R}^{N}$, let us denote by $\kappa
_{\varepsilon}$ the counterpart of the functional introduced in  
\eqref{BMO-type} adequately adapted to $Q_{0}$ (in the sense that elements
of $\mathcal{F}_{\varepsilon}$ are contained in $Q_{0}$). Motivated by the $ 
\Gamma $-convergence formula \eqref{ARBDN}, the variational analysis of the
following functionals has been carried out in \cite{GuS}: 
\begin{equation*}
\mathfrak{F}_{\varepsilon}(u)=\Vert f-u\Vert_{L^{q}(Q_{0})}^{q}+\kappa
_{\varepsilon}(u), 
\end{equation*}
where $q\in\lbrack1,\infty)$. If $q>1$ then \cite[Theorem 1.3]{GuS} claims
convergence (up to subfamily) in $L^{q}$ of the unique minimizers of $ 
\mathfrak{F}_{\varepsilon}$ to the unique minimizer of 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert f-u\Vert_{L^{q}(Q_{0})}^{q}+\frac{1}{4}\Vert u\Vert_{\dot{BV}(Q_{0})}. 
\end{equation*}
If $q=1$ there is an analog in terms of near-minimizers, cf. \cite[Theorem
4.1]{GuS}. In particular, the minimization problems \eqref{IP2} and  
\eqref{IP3} can be understood asymptotically
from $\mathfrak{F}_{\varepsilon}$. Here we observe that this result is
intimately connected with a special case of the general phenomenon based on $ 
K$-functionals described in Theorem \ref{Thm7new}. Indeed, let $ 
B=L^{q}(Q_{0}),A_{0}=L^{1}(Q_{0})$ and $A_{1}=\dot{W}^{1,1}(Q_{0})$ (and so $A_0 \cap A_1 = W^{1, 1}(Q_0) \hookrightarrow A_0 = L^1(Q_0)$ compactly) and
recall that the functional $\kappa_{\varepsilon}(f)$ is comparable to $ 
\frac {K(\varepsilon,f;A_{0},A_{1})}{\varepsilon}$ (cf. \eqref{K-Funct-DS})
and $G_{1}(A_{0},A_{1})=\dot{BV}(Q_{0})$ (cf. \eqref{PJ1.1}).
<!-- end remark-17 -->
<!-- theorem-25: body_md -->
\label{Coro8.32} Let $f\in B_{2,\infty}^{\alpha}, \, \alpha \in (0, 1)$. Then 
\begin{equation}\label{27.9.3}
\Vert\bar{u}-\bar{u}_{n}\Vert_{L^{2}}\lesssim \begin{cases} \|f\|_{\dot{B}^\alpha_{2, \infty}} n^{-\alpha} & \qquad  \text{if}\qquad  \alpha \geq \frac{1}{2}, \\
	(\|\bar{u}\|_{L^\infty} \emph{TV}(\bar{u}))^{1/4}	\|f\|_{\dot{B}^\alpha_{2, \infty}}^{1/2} n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2} -\frac{1}{4}} & \qquad  \text{if} \qquad  \alpha < \frac{1}{2}.
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
Furthermore, these estimates are optimal in the following sense:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] If $\alpha \in [\frac{1}{2}, 1)$ then there exists $f \in B^{\alpha}_{2, \infty}$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{4.10.4}
	\|\bar{u}-\bar{u}_n\|_{L^2}^2 \approx n^{-\alpha}, \qquad \forall n \geq 4. 
\end{equation}
\item[(ii)] If $\alpha \in (0, \frac{1}{2})$ then  there exists $n_0 \in \mathbb{N}$ such that   \begin{equation}\label{1.10.5}
			 	\sup_{f \in \mathcal{K}} \|\bar{u}-\bar{u}_n\|_{L^2} \approx n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2} - \frac{1}{4}}, \qquad \forall n \geq n_0, 
			 \end{equation}
			 where
			 \begin{equation}\label{1.10.3}
			 \mathcal{K} = \{f \in L^2: \|f\|_{\dot{B}^\alpha_{2, \infty}} \leq C, \quad \|\bar{u}\|_{L^\infty} \leq  1, \quad \emph{TV}(\bar{u}) \leq 2 \}
			 \end{equation}
			 for a certain constant $C > 0$ that is independent of $n$. 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-25 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Applying Theorem \ref{Thm1Intro} with $p=2$ and the characterization of the $K
$-functional for $X=L^{2}$ and $k=1$ given in \eqref{PD28.1} we obtain (to simplify the exposition we shall omit the underlying equivalence constants that depend on $f$ and $\bar{u}$)
\begin{align}
\Vert\bar{u}-\bar{u}_{n}\Vert_{L^{2}} &\lesssim K(n^{-1},f;L^{2},\dot{W} 
^{1,2})^{1/2} \min \{n^{-1/4}, K(n^{-1}, f; L^2, \dot{W}^{1, 2})^{1/2}\} \nonumber \\
&\approx \omega(f, n^{-1})_2^{1/2} \min \{n^{-1/4}, \omega(f, n^{-1})_2^{1/2}\}. 
\label{PF19.12}
\end{align}
Combining \eqref{PF19.12} with the elementary estimates 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{B}_{2,\infty}^{\alpha}}=\sup_{h \neq0}\frac{\Vert\Delta
_{h}f\Vert_{L^{2}}}{|h|^{\alpha}}\geq\sup_{0<|h|<n^{-1}}\frac{\Vert\Delta_{h}f 
\Vert_{L^{2}}}{|h|^{\alpha}}\geq
n^{\alpha}\sup_{|h|<n^{-1}}\Vert\Delta_{h}f\Vert_{L^{2}} = n^\alpha \omega(f, n^{-1})_2
\end{equation*}
we arrive at the following estimate
$$
	\Vert\bar{u}-\bar{u}_{n}\Vert_{L^{2}}  \lesssim n^{-\alpha/2} \min \{n^{-1/4}, n^{-\alpha/2}\}.
$$

Next we focus on the sharpness assertion of \eqref{27.9.3}.   We will restrict our attention to the case $\alpha \neq 1/2$, since the optimality with $\alpha = 1/2$ is a well-know result (cf. \cite[Remark 6.6]{BNS} and \cite[Remark 4.7]{KSe}). Assume first that  $\alpha \in (1/2, 1)$ and consider the prototypical example of extremal local singularity in $B^\alpha_{2, \infty}$  given by (cf. \cite[Lemma 1, p. 44]{RS})
	$$
		\psi(x_1) =(\text{sgn } x_1) |x_1|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}
	$$
	for $|x_1|$ sufficiently small (say $|x_1| \leq 1/8$). Let 
	$$
		g_1(x_1) = \begin{cases}
			\psi (\frac{1}{8}) &\text {if} \qquad x_1 \in [0, \frac{1}{4}], \\
			\varphi(x_1) & \text{if} \qquad x_1 \in [\frac{1}{4}, \frac{1}{2}], \\
			\psi(-\frac{1}{8}) & \text{if} \qquad x_1 \in [\frac{1}{2}, \frac{3}{4}], \\
			\psi(x_1-\frac{7}{8}) & \text{if} \qquad x_1 \in [\frac{3}{4}, 1],
		\end{cases}
	$$
			where $\varphi \in C^\infty$
			 is decreasing with  $\varphi(\frac{1}{4}) = \psi(\frac{1}{8}) > 0$ and $\varphi(\frac{1}{2}) = \psi(-\frac{1}{8}) < 0$. Let $g(x) = g_1(x_1)$ for $x = (x_1, x_2) \in \mathbb{T}^2$. Assume that $t \in (0, 1/8)$. By construction, the sign of $g_1'$ does not change  on any interval of length $t$ and then 
			 $$
			 	|g_1(x_1+t)-g_1(x_1)| = \bigg|\int_{x_1}^{x_1 + t} g_1'(r) \, dr \bigg| = \int_{x_1}^{x_1+t} |g_1'(r)| \, dr
			 $$
			 leading to 
			 $$
			 	\|\Delta_{t e_1} g\|_{L^1(\mathbb{T}^2)} = \|\Delta_t g_1\|_{L^1(\mathbb{T})} =  \int_0^1  \int_{0}^{t} |g_1'(x_1 + r)| \, dr \, d x_1 = t \|g_1'\|_{L^1(\mathbb{T})}.
			 $$
			 Here, we note that $\|g_1'\|_{L^1(\mathbb{T})} < \infty$ since $\alpha > 1/2$. Moreover, it is clear that $\Delta_{t e_2} g(x) =0$. Hence
			 $$
			 	\text{TV}(g) = \lim_{t \to 0^{+}} t^{-1} \|\Delta_{t e_1} g\|_{L^1(\mathbb{T}^2)} =  \|g_1'\|_{L^1(\mathbb{T})}. 
			 $$
			Let $z_1 \in C^\infty(\mathbb{T})$ with $|z_1| \leq 1$ such that $z_1 \equiv -1$ on $[\frac{1}{4}, \frac{1}{2}]$ and $z_1 \equiv 1$ on $[\frac{3}{4}, 1]$. Then we define $z(x) = (z_1(x_1), 0)$ and $\xi(x) = - \text{div } z(x) = -z_1'(x_1)$. Then
			 $$
			 	\langle \xi, g \rangle = - \int_0^1 z_1'(x_1) g_1(x_1) \, dx_1 = \int_0^1 z_1(x_1) g_1'(x_1) \, dx_1 = \|g_1'\|_{L^1(\mathbb{T})}  = \text{TV} (g). 
			 $$
			 According to the well-known dual characterization of minimizers (see e.g. \cite[(2.11)]{CCCNP}), we derive that  $g$ is the unique minimizer of \eqref{PF18.3} with data $f = g + \xi$. Furthermore, using that $\psi \in B^\alpha_{2, \infty}$, it is easy  to check that $g_1 \in B^\alpha_{2, \infty}(\mathbb{T})$ and, in particular, $g \in B^\alpha_{2, \infty}(\mathbb{T}^2)$. Then, taking into account that $\xi \in C^\infty(\mathbb{T}^2)$, we also derive that  $f \in B^\alpha_{2, \infty}(\mathbb{T}^2)$. On the other hand, from the fact that $P_n$ is an orthogonal projection onto $X_n$, we infer that
			 \begin{equation}\label{27.9.1}
			 	\|g-\bar{u}_n\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^2)} \geq \inf_{u \in X_n} \|g-u\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^2)} = \|g-P_n g\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^2)}. 
			 \end{equation}
			 Suppose now that $n \geq 4$ and let $I^*_n$ be the unique interval  of the form  $I_{n, i} = [\frac{i-1}{n}, \frac{i}{n}]$ with $1 \leq i \leq n$ that contains $7/8$, that is, there exists $a \in [0, 1/n)$ such that $I^{*}_n = [\frac{7}{8}-a, \frac{7}{8}-a+\frac{1}{n})$. Then 
			 \begin{align}
			 \|g-P_n g\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^2)}^2 &\geq \sum_{i=1}^n  \int_{I^*_n \times I_{n, i}} |g(x)-P_n g(x)|^2 \, dx \nonumber \\
			 &  =  \int_{I^*_n } |g_1(x_1)-(g_1)_{I_n^*}|^2 \, dx_1  \nonumber \\
			 & =  \bigg[ \int_{I_n^*} g^2 - \frac{1}{|I^*_n|}\bigg( \int_{I_n^*} g  \bigg)^2 \bigg] \nonumber \\
			 &  = \frac{1}{n^{2 \alpha}} \bigg[ \int_{-n a}^{-n a +1} |x_1|^{2\alpha -1} \, dx_1 - \bigg( \int_{-n a}^{-n a+1} (\text{sgn } x_1)  |x_1|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}} \, dx_1  \bigg)^2 \bigg] \approx \frac{1}{n^{2 \alpha}}, \label{27.9.2}
			 \end{align}
			 where the last step follows from periodicity and the fact that $\alpha \neq 1/2$. As a combination of \eqref{27.9.1} and \eqref{27.9.2}, we get
			 $$
			 	\|g -\bar{u}_n\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^2)} \gtrsim n^{-\alpha}.
			 $$
			 This proves that \eqref{4.10.4} holds for some $f \in B^\alpha_{2, \infty}(\mathbb{T}^2)$ with $\alpha > 1/2$ and $\bar{u}= g$.  
			 
			 	
			 
Next we turn our attention to \eqref{1.10.5}. 			Let $h = 1/n$ and $\iota = (\lfloor{n/2} \rfloor + 1/2) h$.  Define $\varepsilon = (2 j +1) \frac{h}{2}$, where $j$ is the largest integer such that $\varepsilon \leq h^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2}}$. The existence of such a value $j$ is guaranteed by the assumption $\alpha < 1/2$. Without loss of generality (taking $n \geq n_0$ for some $n_0$ sufficiently large if necessary), we may assume that   $\iota + \varepsilon < 5/6$. Let $\varphi: [0, 1] \longrightarrow \mathbb{R}$ be the piecewise linear function that interpolates the points 
			 $$
			 	(0, 2), \quad \Big(\iota-\frac{h}{2}, \varepsilon \Big), \quad (\iota, 0),  \quad \Big(\iota+\frac{h}{2}, \varepsilon - \frac{h}{2} \Big), \quad (\iota+\varepsilon, 0), \quad (1, 2)
			 $$
			 and $z_1 = 1 -\varphi$. Since $0 \leq \varphi \leq 2$,  we have $|z| \leq 1$ for $z(x) = (z_1(x_1), 0)$. Define
			 $$
			 	f_n(x) = (\mathbf{1}_{[\iota, 1)} + \varphi') (x_1) = (\mathbf{1}_{[\iota, 1)} - z') (x_1),
			 $$
			 or equivalently,  
			 $$
			 	f_n(x) = \sum_{i=1}^5 c_i \mathbf{1}_{J_i}(x_1),
			 $$
			 where
			 $$
			 c_1 = \frac{\varepsilon-2}{\iota-\frac{h}{2}}, \qquad c_2  = -\frac{2 \varepsilon}{h}, \qquad c_3= \frac{2 \varepsilon}{h}, \qquad c_4 = 0, \qquad c_5 = \frac{2}{1-\iota-\varepsilon} + 1, 
			 $$
			 and 
			 $$
			 	J_1 = \Big(0, \iota-\frac{h}{2} \Big), \quad J_2 = \Big(\iota-\frac{h}{2}, \iota \Big), \quad J_3 = \Big(\iota, \iota+ \frac{h}{2} \Big), 
			 $$  
			 $$
			 	J_4 = \Big( \iota+ \frac{h}{2}, \iota + \varepsilon \Big), \quad J_5 = (\iota+\varepsilon, 1). 
			 $$
			 We also let 
			 $$
			 	g_n(x) = \mathbf{1}_{[\iota, 1)}(x_1). 
			 $$
			 Clearly
			 \begin{equation}\label{1.10.2}
			   \|g_n\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb{T}^2)} = 1 \qquad \text{and} \qquad \text{TV}(g_n) = 2.
			   \end{equation}
			    Moreover, we have 
			 $$
			 	\langle -z_1', g_n  \rangle = z_1(\iota)-z_1(1) = \varphi(1)-\varphi(\iota) = 2 = \text{TV} (g_n), 
			 $$
			 which shows that $g_n$ is the unique minimizer of \eqref{PF18.3} with data $f_n$. 
			 
			 To estimate $\|f_n\|_{\dot{B}^\alpha_{2, \infty}}$, we apply that
			 $$
			 	\|\Delta_{\xi} f_n\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^2)} \leq \sum_{i=1}^5 |c_i| \|\Delta_{\xi_1} \mathbf{1}_{J_i}\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T})} \lesssim \sum_{i=1}^5 |c_i| \min\{|\xi_1|, |J_i|\}^{1/2}
			 $$
			 and we next estimate each of the terms in the latter sum. If $i=1$, we use that $\iota-\frac{h}{2} \geq \frac{1}{6}$ (if $n_0$ is large enough)  to derive that $|c_1| \lesssim 1$ uniformly with respect to $n$. If $i=5$ then $|c_5| = \frac{2}{1-\iota-\varepsilon} + 1 \lesssim 1$ since $\iota+\varepsilon < \frac{5}{6}$. Hence, if $|\xi| \leq 1$ then 
			 $$
			 	\|\Delta_{\xi} f_n\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^2)}  \lesssim |\xi_1|^{1/2} + \frac{\varepsilon}{h} \min \Big\{|\xi_1|, \frac{h}{2} \Big\}^{1/2} \lesssim  |\xi_1|^{1/2} + h^{\alpha-1/2} \min \Big\{|\xi_1|, \frac{h}{2} \Big\}^{1/2} \lesssim |\xi|^\alpha. 
			 $$ 
			 This together with the fact that $\|f_n\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^2)} \lesssim 1$ uniformly with respect to $n$ yields that 
			 \begin{equation}\label{1.10.4}
			 \sup_{n \geq n_0} \|f_n\|_{\dot{B}^\alpha_{2, \infty}} < \infty. 
			 \end{equation}
			 It follows from \eqref{1.10.2} and \eqref{1.10.4} that $f_n \in \mathcal{K}$ (cf. \eqref{1.10.3}) for every $n \geq n_0$. 
			 
			 
			 Note that, by construction, the points $\iota-\frac{h}{2}, \iota + \frac{h}{2}$ and $\iota + \varepsilon$ are  of the form $\frac{i}{n}$ for some $1 \leq i \leq n-1$ and then 
			 $$
			 	P_n f_n (x) = c_1 \mathbf{1}_{J_1}(x_1) + c_5 \mathbf{1}_{J_5}(x_1). 
			 $$
			 We are going to prove that $$\bar{u}_n(x) = \frac{\varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon} \,  \mathbf{1}_{[0, \iota + \varepsilon)}(x_1) + \mathbf{1}_{[\iota+\varepsilon, 1)} (x_1)$$
			 is the unique minimizer of \eqref{PF18.3} with data $P_n f_n$. Note that $\bar{u}_n \in X_n$. Let $\zeta_1: [0, 1] \longrightarrow \mathbb{R}$ be such that $\zeta_1(0)=-1$ and
			 \begin{align*}
			 \zeta_1' (x_1)&= \bar{u}_n(x_1, 0)-P_n f_n(x_1, 0) \\
			 & = \Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon}-c_1 \Big) \mathbf{1}_{J_1}(x_1) + \frac{\varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon} \,  \mathbf{1}_{J_2 \cup J_3 \cup J_4}(x_1) + (1-c_5) \mathbf{1}_{J_5}(x_1)
			 \end{align*}
			 or equivalently
			 $$
			 	\zeta_1(x_1) -\zeta_1(0) = \begin{cases}
				\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon}-c_1 \big)x_1 & \quad \text{if} \quad x_1 \in J_1, \\
					 \big(\frac{\varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon}-c_1 \big) \big(\iota-\frac{h}{2} \big) + \frac{\varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon} \big(x_1 -\iota+\frac{h}{2} \big) & \quad \text{if} \quad x_1 \in J_2 \cup J_3 \cup J_4, \\
				  \big(\frac{\varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon}-c_1 \big) \big(\iota-\frac{h}{2} \big) + \frac{\varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon} \big(\varepsilon +\frac{h}{2} \big)  + (1-c_5) (x_1-\iota-\varepsilon) & \quad \text{if} \quad x_1 \in J_5. 	
				\end{cases}
			 $$
			 Observe that $\zeta_1(1) = \zeta_1(0)=-1, \zeta_1(\iota+\varepsilon)=1$, $\zeta_1$ is increasing on $[0, \iota+\varepsilon]$ and decreasing on $[\iota+\varepsilon, 1]$, so $|\zeta_1(x_1)| \leq 1$.  Let $\zeta(x) = (\zeta_1(x_1), 0)$.  Then $|\zeta(x)| \leq 1$ and, in addition, 
			 $$
			 	\langle - \zeta'_1, \bar{u}_n \rangle = 2 \Big(1- \frac{ \varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon} \Big) = \text{TV}(\bar{u}_n),
			 $$
			 which yields the desired assertion that $\bar{u}_n$ is the minimizer of \eqref{PF18.3} associated to $P_n f_n$. 
			 
			 Finally we estimate the error between  the continuous solution $g_n$ and the approximated solution, namely, 
			 \begin{equation}\label{1.10.1}
			 	\|g_n - \bar{u}_n\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^2)}^2  = \bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon} \bigg)^2 \iota + \bigg(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{\iota+\varepsilon} \bigg)^2 \varepsilon = \frac{\varepsilon \iota}{\iota+\varepsilon} \gtrsim \varepsilon.
			 \end{equation}
			 Since $n \geq n_0$ (recall also that $\alpha < 1/2$) and using the definition of $\varepsilon$, we arrive at the following estimates 
			 $$
			 	\frac{h^{\alpha + \frac{1}{2}}}{2} \leq h^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2}} - h \leq \varepsilon,
			 $$
			and then (by \eqref{1.10.1})
			$$
				\|g_n - \bar{u}_n\|_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^2)}  \gtrsim h^{\frac{\alpha}{2} + \frac{1}{4}} = n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2} - \frac{1}{4}}. 
			$$
			 This completes the proof of the desired assertion \eqref{1.10.5}.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- corollary-7: body_md -->
\label{Thm191.} Let $s\in(0,1)$ and $p\in\lbrack1,2)$. Suppose that $f\in\dot{W}^{s,p}\cap L^{\infty},$ then 
\begin{equation}
\Vert\bar{u}-\bar{u}_{n}\Vert_{L^{2}}\lesssim n^{-\frac{sp}{2}}[(1-s)^{ 
\frac {1}{p}}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{s,p}}]^{\frac{p}{2}}\Vert
f\Vert_{L^{\infty}}^{1-\frac{p}{2}},   \label{P19.12}
\end{equation}
where the implicit constant is independent of $n$ and $s$. As a
consequence, taking limits as $s\rightarrow1^{-}$ and applying \eqref{14.9.4}: 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert\bar{u}-\bar{u}_{n}\Vert_{L^{2}}\lesssim n^{-\frac{p}{2}}\Vert f\Vert_{ 
\dot{W}^{1,p}}^{\frac{p}{2}}\Vert f\Vert_{L^{\infty}}^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\qquad 
\text{if}\qquad p\in(1,2) 
\end{equation*}
and 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert\bar{u}-\bar{u}_{n}\Vert_{L^{2}}\lesssim n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Vert f\Vert_{ 
\dot{BV}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Vert f\Vert_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}} \qquad \text{if} \qquad p=1. 
\end{equation*}
<!-- end corollary-7 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
We have the trivial estimates 
\begin{equation}
\Vert f-P_{n}f\Vert_{L^{2}}\leq\Vert
f-P_{n}f\Vert_{L^{p}}^{1-\theta} \|f-P_n f\|_{L^\infty}^\theta \leq 2^\theta  \Vert
f-P_{n}f\Vert_{L^{p}}^{1-\theta}  \Vert f\Vert_{L^{\infty}}^{\theta}, 
\label{PF19.3}
\end{equation}
where $P_{n}$ is the projection operator defined by \eqref{PF19.2} and $\theta = 1-\frac{p}{2}$. 
Moreover, by construction, 
\begin{equation}
\Vert f-P_{n}f\Vert_{L^{p}}^{p}=\sum_{0\leq i,j\leq n-1}\Vert
\mathcal{T}_{S_{i,j}^{h}}f\Vert_{L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h})}^{p}   \label{PF19.4}
\end{equation}
where 
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal{T}_{S_{i,j}^{h}}f(x):=f(x)-f_{S^h_{i, j}}. 
\end{equation*}
On the other hand, the classical Poincar\'{e} inequality applied to the
square $S_{i,j}^{h}$ implies 
\begin{equation}
\Vert \mathcal{T}_{S_{i,j}^{h}}f\Vert_{L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h})}\lesssim h\Vert f\Vert _{ 
\dot{W}^{1,p}(S_{i,j}^{h})}.   \label{PF19.5}
\end{equation}
Interpolating \eqref{PF19.5} with the trivial estimate 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert \mathcal{T}_{S_{i,j}^{h}}f\Vert_{L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h})}\lesssim\Vert f\Vert
_{L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h})},   
\end{equation*}
we arrive at 
\begin{equation}
[
\mathcal{T}_{S_{i,j}^{h}}f]_{(L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h}),L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h}))_{s,p}} 
\lesssim[ f]_{(L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h}), h\dot{W} 
^{1,p}(S_{i,j}^{h}))_{s,p}},   \label{PF19.7}
\end{equation}
where $[\cdot]_{(A_0, A_1)_{s, p}}$ refers to the modified interpolation\footnote{ We mention that  \eqref{5.10.2} plays an important role in extrapolation theory as can be seen in \cite{JM, KM, M-Lim}. In particular, working with ordered pairs $(A_0, A_1)$, one has that (cf. \cite[Lemma 3]{M-Lim})   $$[f]_{(A_0, A_1)_{s, p}} \leq \|f\|_{(A_0, A_1)_{s, p}} \leq (1+s^{-1/p} (1-s)^{1/p}) [f]_{(A_0, A_1)_{s, p}}.$$}   (semi-)norm (cf. \eqref{5.10.1}) given by 
\begin{equation}\label{5.10.2}
	 [f]_{(A_0, A_1)_{s,p}} := \bigg(\int_0^1 [t^{-s}K(t, f; A_0, A_1)]^p \, \frac{dt}{t}	 \bigg)^{1/p}
\end{equation}
 and the hidden equivalence constant in \eqref{PF19.7} is independent of $h$ and $S^h_{i, j}$ with $0\leq
i,j\leq n-1$. By virtue of \eqref{sec2:3}, we can compute the left-hand side
of \eqref{PF19.7} as 
\begin{equation}
[
\mathcal{T}_{S_{i,j}^{h}}f]_{(L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h}),L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h}))_{s,p}}= ((1-s)p)^{-1/p} \|
\mathcal{T}_{S_{i,j}^{h}}f\|_{L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h})}.   \label{PF19.9}
\end{equation}
Concerning the right-hand side of \eqref{PF19.7}, we can make use of the
simple fact that\footnote{ 
More generally, a simple change of variables gives  $[ f]
_{(A_{0},hA_{1})_{s,p}} =  h^{s}\big( \int_0^h [t^{-s}K(t, f; A_0, A_1)]^p \, \frac{dt}{t} \big)^{1/p}$ for every $ 
h > 0$.} 
\begin{equation}
[ f]_{(L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h}),h\dot{W}^{1,p}(S_{i,j}^{h}))_{s,p}} =  h^{s} \bigg( \int_0^h [t^{-s} K(t, f; L^p(S^h_{i, j}), \dot{W}^{1, p}(S_{i, j}^h))]^p \, \frac{dt}{t} \bigg)^{1/p}
\label{P19.10}
\end{equation}
and, in addition, the computations carried out in \eqref{PD28.3} (more
precisely, their counterparts adapted to cubes which hold \textit{mutatis
mutandis}, in particular, the formula  \eqref{PD28.1} remains valid if $t \in (0, h)$; we refer to \cite[p. 17]{DLTYY} for further details) show that 
\begin{equation*}
\bigg( \int_0^h [t^{-s} K(t, f; L^p(S^h_{i, j}), \dot{W}^{1, p}(S_{i, j}^h))]^p \, \frac{dt}{t} \bigg)^{1/p} \approx 
\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{s,p}(S_{i,j}^{h})}
\end{equation*}
with equivalence constants that are independent of $s \in (0, 1)$ and $S_{i,j}^{h}$.
Hence \eqref{P19.10} yields 
\begin{equation}
[ f]_{(L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h}),h\dot{W}^{1,p}(S_{i,j}^{h}))_{s,p}}\approx
h^{s}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{s,p}(S_{i,j}^{h})}.   \label{P19.11}
\end{equation}
Plugging \eqref{PF19.9} and \eqref{P19.11} into \eqref{PF19.7}, we obtain 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert \mathcal{T}_{S_{i,j}^{h}}f\Vert_{L^{p}(S_{i,j}^{h})}\lesssim ((1-s)p)^{1/p} h^{s}\Vert
f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{s,p}(S_{i,j}^{h})}
\end{equation*}
and then \eqref{PF19.4} gives 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert f-P_{n}f\Vert_{L^{p}}\lesssim (1-s)^{1/p}h^{s}\bigg(\sum_{0\leq i,j\leq
n-1}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{s,p}(S_{i,j}^{h})}^{p}\bigg)^{1/p}\leq
(1-s)^{1/p}h^{s}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W}^{s,p}}. 
\end{equation*}
The last estimate combined with \eqref{PF19.3} yields 
\begin{equation*}
\Vert f-P_{n}f\Vert_{L^{2}} \lesssim[(1-s)^{1/p}h^{s}\Vert f\Vert_{\dot{W} 
^{s,p}}]^{1-\theta}\Vert f\Vert_{L^{\infty}}^{\theta}. 
\end{equation*}
This together with \eqref{24.9.10} leads to the desired estimate  
\eqref{P19.12}.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
In light of Theorem \ref{Thm1Intro}, the desired estimate \eqref{25.3Nov} can
be seen  as a simple consequence of the following general question: Given $ 
\alpha\in(0,1)$, characterize those $f\in L^{2}$ that satisfy 
\begin{equation}
K(t,f;L^{2},\dot{W}^{1,p})\lesssim t^{\alpha}.   \label{25.2Nov}
\end{equation}
In the language of interpolation theory \eqref{25.2Nov} is equivalent to the
statement 
\begin{equation}
f\in(L^{2},\dot{W}^{1,p})_{\alpha,\infty}.   \label{PF19.13}
\end{equation}
In this way the problem is reduced to characterizing the interpolation spaces
in \eqref{PF19.13}. The Besov spaces $\dot{B}_{2,\infty}^{\alpha}$ provide
the  well-known answer when $p=2$ (cf. Theorem \ref{Coro8.32}). However,
the situation is more delicate when $p\neq2$ where there is no suitable 
characterization of $K(t,f;L^{2},\dot{W}^{1,p}).$ Despite this obstruction,
the interpolation spaces arising in \eqref{PF19.13} with $p \neq 2$ have been recently
described in \cite{DSSVSY} in terms of the functionals \eqref{BVSY} 
. To be more precise, according to \cite[Corollary 1.15]{DSSVSY} (note that the arguments carried out in \cite{DSSVSY} refer to $\mathbb{R}^N$, but similar arguments also work in the periodic setting) the following interpolation formula holds 
\begin{equation}
(L^{2},\dot{W}^{1,p})_{\alpha,\infty}=\dot{B}_{q}^{\alpha}(\gamma ,\infty) 
\label{PF20.1}
\end{equation}
where $\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1-\alpha}{2}+\frac{\alpha}{p}$ and $\frac{1}{\gamma 
}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}.$ Then, by \eqref{enlaprimera}, 
\begin{equation}
\Vert\bar{u}-\bar{u}_{n}\Vert_{L^{2}}\lesssim K(n^{-2+\frac{2}{p}},f;L^{2}, 
\dot{W}^{1,p})\lesssim n^{-(2-\frac{2}{p})\alpha} \|f\|_{\dot{B}_{q}^{\alpha}(\gamma,\infty)}   \label{PF20.2}
\end{equation}
provided that $\alpha \geq \frac{p}{4(p-1)}$. 
 Note that, in view
of \eqref{PF20.1} and the Sobolev embedding theorem, 
\begin{equation*}
\dot{B}_{q}^{\alpha}(\gamma,\infty)\subset L^{2}+\dot{W}^{1,p}\subset L^{2}. 
\end{equation*}
Then \eqref{25.3Nov} with $\alpha \geq 1/2$ follows after applying a simple change of variables ($\beta \leftrightarrow \alpha$) in  
\eqref{PF20.2}. The case $\alpha < 1/2$ in \eqref{25.3Nov} can be obtained similarly.  In addition, the validity of \eqref{25.3Nov} for $f\in\dot {W 
}^{\beta,q}$ is an immediate consequence of $\dot {W} 
^{\beta,q}\hookrightarrow\dot{B}_{q}^{\beta}(\gamma,\infty)$, which easily follows from the trivial embedding  $L^p(\nu_\gamma) \hookrightarrow L^{p, \infty}(\nu_\gamma)$.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
